**Sri Lanka**,[^cite_note-16] officially the **Democratic Socialist Republic of Sri Lanka**, formerly known as **Ceylon**,[^cite_note-17] is an [island country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Island_country) in [South Asia](21566765.md). It is located in the [Indian Ocean](14580.md), southwest of the [Bay of Bengal](47892.md), and is separated from [India](14533.md) by the [Gulf of Mannar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Mannar) and the [Palk Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palk_Strait). Sri Lanka shares a [maritime border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_border) with the [Maldives](19117.md) to the southwest and India to the northwest, and lies across the Bay of Bengal from [Bangladesh](3454.md) and [Myanmar](19457.md) to the northeast and the [Andaman and Nicobar Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andaman_and_Nicobar_Islands) of India to the east. Its executive and judicial capital is [Colombo](56636.md), which is also its [largest city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Sri_Lanka) and the political, financial and cultural centre of the country; neighboring [Sri Jayawardenepura Kotte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Jayawardenepura_Kotte) is the legislative capital. Sri Lanka's [population is 22 million](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Sri_Lanka). About 74% of the population are [Sinhalese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhalese_people), who speak the [Sinhala language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhala_language), and about 15% are [Tamils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Tamils) who speak the [Tamil language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language). Other long-established ethnic groups include the [Moors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Moors), [Indian Tamils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Tamils_of_Sri_Lanka), [Burghers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burgher_people), [Malays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Malays), [Chinese](29408939.md), and [Vedda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vedda).[^cite_note-18]

The island has a documented history of over 3,000 years, with evidence of [prehistoric human settlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_human_migrations) dating back 125,000 years.[^cite_note-histr-19] Sri Lanka has been given [various names](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_Sri_Lanka) throughout its long history, with Ceylon most notably being used prior to its independence in 1948.[^cite_note-20] The earliest known [Buddhist writings in the island](1164480.md), known collectively as the [Pali Canon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pali_Canon), date back to [29 BCE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Therav%C4%81da_Council).[^cite_note-21][^cite_note-22] Sri Lanka, owing to its strategic geographical location, played a role as a major trading hub, and was well known to explorers across the world as early as the [Anuradhapura period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anuradhapura_period). The [Portuguese Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Empire) established [a colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Ceylon) in the sixteenth century, during a period of [political upheaval](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crisis_of_the_Sixteenth_Century) in the [Kingdom of Kotte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kotte) where it also faced attacks from neighbouring [kingdoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhala_kingdom) of [Kandy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kandy) and [Sitawaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Sitawaka). Following the [Sinhalese–Portuguese War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhalese%E2%80%93Portuguese_War), the [Dutch colonial empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_colonial_empire) controlled the [coastal areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_Ceylon). By the early 19th century, the [British Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Empire) established a [colony on the island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Ceylon), which lasted until 1948. The early 20th century saw the rise of nationalist movements and [increasing calls for independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_independence_movement). In 1948, Sri Lanka gained independence as the [Dominion of Ceylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominion_of_Ceylon), eventually becoming a [republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic) in 1972. Sri Lanka's recent history has been marred by a [prolonged civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_civil_war) between the Tamil separatist militant organisation of [Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam) and the [Sri Lankan Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Armed_Forces), which lasted from 1983 to 2009, and ended with the [defeat of the separatists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eelam_War_IV).[^cite_note-23]

In the 21st century, Sri Lanka has emerged as a [developing country](78449.md), with a strategically important geographical location in the Indian Ocean, with its deep harbours giving it significant geopolitical leverage as a major trading post from the ancient [Maritime Silk Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_Silk_Road).[^cite_note-silkr-24][^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26] It has the [highest level of human development in South Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_Human_Development_Index) and the [second-highest GDP per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)_per_capita) in the region. Sri Lanka has a long history of international engagement and cooperation, and is a member of several international organisations—including [SAARC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asian_Association_for_Regional_Cooperation), the [G77](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_of_77), the [Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement) and the [Commonwealth of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations).

## Names and toponymy

Main article: [Names of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_Sri_Lanka)

In antiquity, Sri Lanka was known to travellers by a variety of names. According to the *[Mahāvaṃsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mah%C4%81va%E1%B9%83sa)*, the legendary [Prince Vijaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_Vijaya) named the island [Tambapaṇṇĩ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Tambapanni) ("[copper-red](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copper_\(color\)) hands" or "copper-red earth"), because his followers' hands were reddened by the [red soil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_soil) of the area where he landed.[^cite_note-27][^cite_note-JMS_1997-28] In [Hindu mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_mythology), the term [*Lankā*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lanka) ("Island") appears but it is unknown whether it refers to the island. The Tamil term [Eelam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eelam) ([Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language): ஈழம், romanised: *īḻam*) was used to designate the whole island in [Sangam literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangam_literature).[^cite_note-29][^cite_note-30] The island was known [under Chola rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chola_conquest_of_Anuradhapura) as *Mummudi Cholamandalam* ("realm of the [three crowned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Crowned_Kings) Cholas").[^cite_note-31]

[Ancient Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek) geographers called it *[Taprobanā](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taprobana)* ([Ancient Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek_language): Ταπροβανᾶ) or *Taprobanē* (Ταπροβανῆ)[^cite_note-32] from the word *Tambapanni*. The Arabs and Persians referred to it as *Sarandīb* (the origin of the word "[serendipity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serendipity)") from [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit) *Siṃhaladvīpaḥ*.[^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34] *Ceilão*, the name given to Sri Lanka by the Portuguese when they arrived in 1505,[^cite_note-35] was transliterated into English as *Ceylon*.[^cite_note-36] As a British [crown colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_colony), the island was known as Ceylon; it achieved independence as the [Dominion of Ceylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominion_of_Ceylon) in 1948.

The country is now known in Sinhala as *_Śrī Laṅkā_* ([Sinhala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhala_language): ශ්‍රී ලංකා) and in Tamil as *_Ilaṅkai_* ([Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language): இலங்கை, IPA: [\[iˈlaŋɡaɪ\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Tamil)). In 1972, its formal name was changed to Free, Sovereign and Independent Republic of Sri Lanka. Later, on 7 September 1978, it was changed to the Democratic Socialist Republic of Sri Lanka.[^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38] As the name Ceylon still appears in the names of a number of organisations, the Sri Lankan government announced in 2011 a plan to rename all those over which it has authority.[^cite_note-39]

## History

Main article: [History of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Sri_Lanka)

### Prehistory

Main article: [Prehistory of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prehistory_of_Sri_Lanka)

The pre-history of Sri Lanka goes back 125,000 years and possibly even as far back as 500,000 years.[^cite_note-pps-40] The era spans the [Palaeolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palaeolithic), [Mesolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesolithic), and early [Iron Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_Age). Among the Palaeolithic human settlements discovered in Sri Lanka, [Pahiyangala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fa_Hien_Cave) (37,000 BP), named after the [Chinese](372983.md) traveller [monk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhikkhu) [Faxian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faxian);[^cite_note-41] [Batadombalena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batadombalena) (28,500 BP);[^cite_note-btd-42] and [Belilena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belilena) (12,000 BP) are the most important. In these caves, archaeologists have found the remains of [anatomically](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anatomy) modern [humans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humans) which they have named [Balangoda Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balangoda_Man), and other evidence[^cite_note-43] suggesting that they may have engaged in [agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture) and kept domestic dogs for driving game.[^cite_note-44]

The earliest inhabitants of Sri Lanka were likely ancestors of the [Vedda people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vedda_people),[^cite_note-45] an indigenous people numbering approximately 2,500 living in modern-day Sri Lanka.

During the protohistoric period (1000–500 BCE) Sri Lanka was culturally united with southern India,[^cite_note-46] and shared the same megalithic burials, [pottery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_and_red_ware_culture), iron technology, farming techniques and [megalithic graffiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megalithic_graffiti_symbols).[^cite_note-Seneviratne-47][^cite_note-Karunaratne-48] This cultural complex spread from southern India along with Dravidian clans such as the [Velir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velir), prior to the migration of [Prakrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prakrit) speakers.[^cite_note-49][^cite_note-50][^cite_note-Seneviratne-47]

One of the first written references to the island is found in the Indian [epic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epic_poetry) [Ramayana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramayana), which provides details of a kingdom named *[Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lanka)* that was created by the divine sculptor [Vishvakarma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vishwakarma) for [Kubera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kubera), the God of Wealth.[^cite_note-51] It is said that Kubera was overthrown by his [rakshasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rakshasa) stepbrother, [Ravana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ravana).[^cite_note-parkrsl-52]

### Ancient history

Main articles: [Pre-Anuradhapura period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre-Anuradhapura_period) and [Anuradhapura period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anuradhapura_period)



[Ptolemy's world map](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ptolemy's_world_map) of Ceylon, first century CE, in a 1535 publication

According to the *[Mahāvaṃsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mah%C4%81va%E1%B9%83sa)*, a [Pāḷi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pali) chronicle written in the 5th century CE, the original inhabitants of Sri Lanka are said to be the [Yakshas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaksha) and [Nagas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naga_people_\(Lanka\)). Sinhalese history traditionally starts in 543 BCE with the arrival of [Prince Vijaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_Vijaya), a semi-legendary prince who sailed with 700 followers to Sri Lanka, after being expelled from [Vanga kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanga_kingdom) (present-day [Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal)).[^cite_note-53] He established the [Kingdom of Tambapanni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Tambapanni), near modern-day [Mannar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mannar,_Sri_Lanka). Vijaya (Singha) is the first of the approximately [189 monarchs of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Sri_Lankan_monarchs) described in chronicles such as the *[Dīpavaṃsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C4%ABpava%E1%B9%83sa)*, *Mahāvaṃsa*, *[Cūḷavaṃsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C5%AB%E1%B8%B7ava%E1%B9%83sa)*, and *Rājāvaliya*.[^cite_note-54]

Once [Prakrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prakrit) speakers had attained dominance on the island, the *[Mahāvaṃsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mah%C4%81va%E1%B9%83sa)* further recounts the later migration of royal brides and service castes from the Tamil [Pandya kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandya_kingdom) to the [Anuradhapura kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anuradhapura_kingdom) in the early historic period.[^cite_note-55]



The [Avukana Buddha statue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avukana_Buddha_statue), a 12-metre-tall (39 ft) standing Buddha statue from the reign of [Dhatusena of Anuradhapura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhatusena_of_Anuradhapura), 5th century

The [Anuradhapura period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anuradhapura_period) (377 BCE – 1017 CE) began with the establishment of the [Anuradhapura kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anuradhapura_kingdom) in 380 BCE during the reign of [Pandukabhaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandukabhaya). Thereafter, Anuradhapura served as the capital city of the country for nearly 1,400 years.[^cite_note-56] Ancient Sri Lankans excelled at building certain types of [structures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_constructions_of_Sri_Lanka) such as [tanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_irrigation_network), [dagobas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stupa) and palaces.[^cite_note-57] Society underwent a major transformation during the reign of [Devanampiya Tissa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devanampiya_Tissa), with the arrival of Buddhism from India. In 250 BCE,[^cite_note-58] [Mahinda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahinda_\(Buddhist_monk\)), a [bhikkhu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhikkhu) and the son of the [Mauryan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurya_Empire) Emperor [Ashoka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashoka) arrived in [Mihintale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mihintale) carrying the message of Buddhism.[^cite_note-Macmillan1-59] His mission won over the monarch, who embraced the faith and propagated it throughout the [Sinhalese population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhalese_people).[^cite_note-mahav1-60]

Succeeding kingdoms of Sri Lanka would maintain many [Buddhist schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schools_of_Buddhism) and monasteries and support the propagation of Buddhism into other countries in Southeast Asia. Sri Lankan Bhikkhus studied in India's famous ancient Buddhist University of [Nalanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nalanda), which was destroyed by [Bakhtiyar Khalji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Bakhtiyar_Khalji). It is probable that many of the scriptures from Nalanda are preserved in Sri Lanka's many monasteries and that the written form of the [Tripiṭaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tripi%E1%B9%ADaka), including Sinhalese Buddhist literature, were part of the University of Nalanda.[^cite_note-61] In 245 BCE, [bhikkhunī](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhikkhun%C4%AB) [Sanghamitta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanghamitta) arrived with the [Jaya Sri Maha Bodhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaya_Sri_Maha_Bodhi) tree, which is considered to be a sapling from the historical [Bodhi Tree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bodhi_Tree) under which [Gautama Buddha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gautama_Buddha) became enlightened.[^cite_note-62] It is considered the oldest human-planted tree (with a continuous historical record) in the world. ([Bodhivaṃsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bodhiva%E1%B9%83sa))[^cite_note-63]

Sri Lanka experienced the first of many foreign invasions during the reign of [Suratissa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suratissa_of_Anuradhapura), who was defeated by two horse traders named [Sena and Guttika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sena_and_Guttika) from South India.[^cite_note-mahav1-60] The next invasion came immediately in 205 BCE by a [Chola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chola) named [Elara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elara_\(monarch\)), who overthrew [Asela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asela_of_Anuradhapura) and ruled the country for 44 years. [Dutugamunu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutugamunu), the eldest son of the southern regional sub-king, [Kavan Tissa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kavan_Tissa,_Prince_of_Ruhuna), defeated Elara in the [Battle of Vijithapura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Vijithapura). During its two and a half millennia of existence, the [Sinhala kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhala_kingdom) was invaded at least eight times by neighbouring South Indian dynasties such as the [Chola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chola_dynasty), [Pandya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandya_dynasty), and [Pallava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallava_dynasty). There also were incursions by the kingdoms of [Kalinga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalinga_\(region\)) (modern [Odisha](250724.md)) and from the [Malay Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malay_Peninsula) as well.



The [Sigiriya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigiriya) ("Lion Rock"), a rock fortress and city, built by King [Kashyapa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashyapa_I_of_Anuradhapura) (477–495 CE) as a new more defensible capital. It was also used as a Buddhist monastery after the capital was moved back to [Anuradhapura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anuradhapura).

The [Fourth Theravāda Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Therav%C4%81da_Council) was held at the [Anuradhapura Maha Viharaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anuradhapura_Maha_Viharaya) in Sri Lanka under the patronage of [Valagamba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valagamba) in 25 BCE. The council was held in response to a year in which the harvests in Sri Lanka were particularly poor and many [Buddhist monks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhikkhu) subsequently died of starvation. Because the [Pali Canon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pali_Canon) was at that time [oral literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oral_literature) maintained in several recensions by *dhammabhāṇaka*s ([dharma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dharma) reciters), the surviving monks recognised the danger of not writing it down so that even if some of the monks whose duty it was to study and remember parts of the Canon for later generations died, the teachings would not be lost.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBuswellLopez2013200-64] After the council, [palm-leaf manuscripts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm-leaf_manuscript) containing the completed Canon were taken to other countries such as [Burma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burma), [Thailand](30128.md), [Cambodia](334751.md) and [Laos](17752.md).

Sri Lanka was the first Asian country known to have a female ruler: [Anula of Anuradhapura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anula_of_Anuradhapura) (r. 47–42 BCE).[^cite_note-sigiriya12-65] Sri Lankan monarchs undertook some remarkable construction projects such as [Sigiriya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigiriya), the so-called "Fortress in the Sky", built during the reign of [Kashyapa I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashyapa_I), who ruled between 477 and 495. The Sigiriya rock fortress is surrounded by an extensive network of ramparts and moats. Inside this protective enclosure were gardens, ponds, pavilions, palaces and other structures.[^cite_note-Ponnamperuma_2013-66][^cite_note-Bandaranayake_1999-67]

In 993 CE, the invasion of [Chola emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chola_dynasty) [Rajaraja I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajaraja_I) forced the then Sinhalese ruler [Mahinda V](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahinda_V) to flee to the southern part of Sri Lanka. Taking advantage of this situation, [Rajendra I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajendra_I), son of Rajaraja I, launched a large invasion in 1017. Mahinda V was captured and taken to India, and the Cholas [sacked the city of Anuradhapura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chola_conquest_of_Anuradhapura) causing the fall of [Anuradhapura kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anuradhapura_kingdom). Subsequently, they moved the capital to [Polonnaruwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polonnaruwa).[^cite_note-Siriweera_2004,_p._44-68]

### Post-classical period

Main articles: [Polonnaruwa period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polonnaruwa_period) and [Transitional period of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transitional_period_of_Sri_Lanka)

Following a 17-year-long campaign, [Vijayabahu I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayabahu_I_of_Polonnaruwa) successfully drove the Chola out of Sri Lanka in 1070, reuniting the country for the first time in over a century.[^cite_note-69][^cite_note-70] Upon his request, ordained monks were sent from [Burma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burma) to Sri Lanka to re-establish Buddhism, which had almost disappeared from the country during the Chola reign.[^cite_note-71] During the mediaeval period, Sri Lanka was divided into three sub-territories, namely, [Ruhunu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Ruhuna), Pihiti and [Maya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maya_Rata).[^cite_note-72]



The seated image of [Gal Vihara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gal_Vihara) in [Polonnaruwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polonnaruwa), 12th century, which depicts the [dhyana mudra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mudras_\(yoga\)), shows signs of [Mahayana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahayana) influence.

Sri Lanka's [irrigation system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_irrigation_network) was extensively expanded during the reign of [Parākramabāhu the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parakramabahu_I_of_Polonnaruwa) (1153–1186).[^cite_note-ethniccr-73] This period is considered as a time when Sri Lanka was at the height of its power.[^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75] He built 1,470 reservoirs – the highest number by any ruler in Sri Lanka's history – repaired 165 dams, 3,910 canals, 163 major reservoirs, and 2,376 mini-reservoirs.[^cite_note-ethniccr1-76] His most famous construction is the [Parakrama Samudra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parakrama_Samudra),[^cite_note-77] the largest irrigation project of mediaeval Sri Lanka. Parākramabāhu's reign is memorable for two major campaigns – in the south of India as part of a Pandyan war of succession, and a punitive strike against the kings of Ramanna ([Burma](19457.md)) for various perceived insults to Sri Lanka.[^cite_note-78]

After his demise, Sri Lanka gradually decayed in power. In 1215, [Kalinga Magha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalinga_Magha), an invader with uncertain origins, identified as the founder of the Jaffna kingdom, invaded and captured the [Kingdom of Polonnaruwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Polonnaruwa). He sailed from [Kalinga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalinga_\(historical_region\))[^cite_note-ethniccr1-76] 690 nautical miles on 100 large ships with a 24,000 strong army. Unlike previous invaders, he [looted](209811.md), ransacked and destroyed everything in the ancient [Anuradhapura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anuradhapura_kingdom) and Polonnaruwa Kingdoms beyond recovery.[^cite_note-79] His priorities in ruling were to extract as much as possible from the land and overturn as many of the traditions of [Rajarata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajarata) as possible. His reign saw the massive migration of native [Sinhalese people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhalese_people) to the south and west of Sri Lanka, and into the mountainous interior, in a bid to escape his power.[^cite_note-Nadarajan,_V_p._72-80][^cite_note-Indrapala,_K_p._16-81]

Sri Lanka never really recovered from the effects of Kalinga Magha's invasion. King Vijayabâhu III, who led the resistance, brought the kingdom to [Dambadeniya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Dambadeniya). The north, in the meanwhile, eventually evolved into the [Jaffna kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaffna_kingdom).[^cite_note-Nadarajan,_V_p._72-80][^cite_note-Indrapala,_K_p._16-81] The Jaffna kingdom never came under the rule of any kingdom of the south except on one occasion; in 1450, following the conquest led by king [Parâkramabâhu VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parakramabahu_VI_of_Kotte)'s adopted son, [Prince Sapumal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhuvanaikabahu_VI_of_Kotte).[^cite_note-82] He ruled the North from 1450 to 1467 CE.[^cite_note-83]

The next three centuries starting from 1215 were marked by kaleidoscopically shifting collections of capitals in south and central Sri Lanka, including Dambadeniya, [Yapahuwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yapahuwa), [Gampola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gampola), [Raigama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raigama), [Kotte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kotte),[^cite_note-HC-84] [Sitawaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Sitawaka), and finally, [Kandy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kandy). In 1247, the Malay kingdom of [Tambralinga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tambralinga) which was a vassal of [Sri Vijaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Vijaya) led by their king [Chandrabhanu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chandrabhanu)[^cite_note-85] briefly invaded Sri Lanka from [Insular Southeast Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insular_Southeast_Asia). They were then expelled by the South Indian Pandyan dynasty.[^cite_note-86] However, this temporary invasion reinforced the steady flow of the presence of various [Austronesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austronesian_people) merchant ethnic groups, from [Sumatrans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Malays) (Indonesia) to [Lucoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines%E2%80%93Sri_Lanka_relations) (Philippines) into Sri Lanka which occurred since 200 BCE.[^cite_note-87] Chinese admiral [Zheng He](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zheng_He) and his naval expeditionary force landed at Galle, Sri Lanka in 1409 and got into [battle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming-Kotte_War) with the local king [Vira Alakesvara of Gampola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vira_Alakesvara_of_Gampola). Zheng He captured King Vira Alakesvara and later released him.[^cite_note-88][^cite_note-89][^cite_note-90][^cite_note-91] Zheng He erected the [Galle Trilingual Inscription](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galle_Trilingual_Inscription), a stone tablet at [Galle](426566.md) written in three [languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages) ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language), [Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language), and [Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language)), to commemorate his visit.[^cite_note-92][^cite_note-93] The [stele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stele) was discovered by S. H. Thomlin at Galle in 1911 and is now preserved in the [Colombo National Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_of_Colombo).

### Early modern period

Main article: [Kandyan period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kandyan_period)

See also: [Portuguese Ceylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Ceylon), [Dutch Ceylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_Ceylon), and [British Ceylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Ceylon)



A 17th-century engraving of [Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_colonial_empire) explorer [Joris van Spilbergen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joris_van_Spilbergen) meeting with King Vimaladharmasuriya in 1602

A 1595 map of Sri Lanka created by Dutch cartographer Petrus Plancius

A 1595 map of Sri Lanka created by [Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_colonial_empire) cartographer [Petrus Plancius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrus_Plancius)

The early modern period of Sri Lanka begins with the arrival of Portuguese soldier and explorer [Lourenço de Almeida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louren%C3%A7o_de_Almeida), the son of [Francisco de Almeida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_de_Almeida), in 1505.[^cite_note-francal-94] In 1517, the Portuguese built a fort at the port city of [Colombo](56636.md) and gradually extended their control over the coastal areas. In 1592, after decades of intermittent warfare with the Portuguese, [Vimaladharmasuriya I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vimaladharmasuriya_I) moved his kingdom to the inland city of [Kandy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kandy), a location he thought more secure from attack.[^cite_note-isl23-95] In 1619, succumbing to attacks by the [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Empire), the independent existence of the [Jaffna kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaffna_kingdom) came to an end.[^cite_note-96]

During the reign of the [Rajasinha II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajasinha_II), Dutch explorers arrived on the island. In 1638, the king signed a [treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kandyan_Treaty_of_1638) with the [Dutch East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_East_India_Company) to get rid of the Portuguese who ruled most of the coastal areas.[^cite_note-97] The following [Dutch–Portuguese War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch%E2%80%93Portuguese_War) resulted in a Dutch victory, with Colombo falling into Dutch hands by 1656. The Dutch remained in the areas they had captured, thereby violating the treaty they had signed in 1638. The [Burgher people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burgher_people), a distinct ethnic group, emerged as a result of intermingling between the Dutch and native Sri Lankans in this period.[^cite_note-98]

The Kingdom of Kandy was the last independent monarchy of Sri Lanka.[^cite_note-kandyk-99] In 1595, Vimaladharmasurya brought the sacred [Tooth Relic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relic_of_the_tooth_of_the_Buddha)—the traditional symbol of royal and religious authority amongst the [Sinhalese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhalese_people)—to Kandy and built the [Temple of the Tooth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_the_Tooth).[^cite_note-kandyk-99] In spite of on-going intermittent warfare with Europeans, the kingdom survived. Later, a crisis of succession emerged in Kandy upon king [Vira Narendra Sinha ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vira_Narendra_Sinha)'s death in 1739. He was married to a [Telugu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_language)-speaking [Nayakkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nayaks_of_Madurai) princess from South India ([Madurai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madurai)) and was childless by her.[^cite_note-kandyk-99]

Eventually, with the support of [bhikkhu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhikkhu) Weliwita Sarankara and ignoring the right of *"Unambuwe Bandara"*, the crown passed to the brother of one of Narendrasinha's princesses, overlooking Narendrasinha's own son by a Sinhalese [concubine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concubinage).[^cite_note-100] The new king was crowned [Sri Vijaya Rajasinha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Vijaya_Rajasinha) later that year. Kings of the Nayakkar dynasty launched several attacks on Dutch controlled areas, which proved to be unsuccessful.[^cite_note-101]



[Sri Vikrama Rajasinha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Vikrama_Rajasinha) of Kandy, the last ruling native Sri Lankan monarch

During the [French Revolutionary Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Revolutionary_Wars), fearing that French control of the Netherlands might deliver Sri Lanka to the French, the [British Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Empire) occupied the coastal areas of the island (which they called the colony of [British Ceylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Ceylon)) with little difficulty in 1796.[^cite_note-colvoyg-102] Two years later, in 1798, [Sri Rajadhi Rajasinha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Rajadhi_Rajasinha), third of the four Nayakkar kings of Sri Lanka, died of a fever. Following his death, a nephew of Rajadhi Rajasinha, eighteen-year-old Kannasamy, was crowned.[^cite_note-scnsl-103] The young king, now named [Sri Vikrama Rajasinha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Vikrama_Rajasinha), faced a British invasion in 1803 but successfully retaliated. The First Kandyan War ended in a stalemate.[^cite_note-scnsl-103]

By then the entire coastal area was under the [British East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_East_India_Company) as a result of the [Treaty of Amiens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Amiens). On 14 February 1815, [Kandy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kandy) was occupied by the British in the [Second Kandyan War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Kandyan_War), ending Sri Lanka's independence.[^cite_note-scnsl-103] Sri Vikrama Rajasinha, the last native monarch of Sri Lanka, was exiled to India.[^cite_note-104] The [Kandyan Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kandyan_Convention) formally ceded the entire country to the British Empire. Attempts by Sri Lankan noblemen to undermine British power in 1818 during the [Uva Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Rebellion_of_1817%E2%80%931818) were thwarted by [Governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governors_of_British_Ceylon) [Robert Brownrigg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Brownrigg).[^cite_note-keppetipola-105]

The beginning of the modern period of Sri Lanka is marked by the [Colebrooke–Cameron reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colebrooke%E2%80%93Cameron_Commission) of 1833.[^cite_note-corankel-106] They introduced a [utilitarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utilitarianism) and [liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberalism) political culture to the country based on the [rule of law](25166191.md) and amalgamated the Kandyan and maritime provinces as a single unit of government.[^cite_note-corankel-106] An [executive council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Council_of_Ceylon) and a [legislative council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_Council_of_Ceylon) were established, later becoming the foundation of a representative legislature. By this time, experiments with [coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee) plantations were largely successful.[^cite_note-Nubin_2002_115-107]

Soon, coffee became the primary commodity export of Sri Lanka. Falling coffee prices as a result of the [depression of 1847](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panic_of_1847) stalled economic development and prompted the governor to introduce a series of taxes on firearms, dogs, shops, boats, etc., and to reintroduce a form of *rajakariya*, requiring six days free labour on roads or payment of a cash equivalent.[^cite_note-Nubin_2002_115-107] These harsh measures antagonised the locals, and [another rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matale_rebellion) broke out in 1848.[^cite_note-matale1-108] A devastating leaf disease, *[Hemileia vastatrix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hemileia_vastatrix)*, struck the coffee plantations in 1869, destroying the entire industry within fifteen years.[^cite_note-109] The British quickly found a replacement: abandoning coffee, they began cultivating tea instead. [Tea production in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea_production_in_Sri_Lanka) thrived in the following decades. Large-scale rubber plantations began in the early 20th century.



British appointed [Kandyan chief headmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_headmen_of_Ceylon) in 1905.

By the end of the 19th century, a new educated [social class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_class) transcending race and [caste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caste) arose through British attempts to staff the [Ceylon Civil Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceylon_Civil_Service) and the legal, educational, engineering, and medical professions with natives.[^cite_note-110] New leaders represented the various ethnic groups of the population in the [Ceylon Legislative Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_Council_of_Ceylon) on a communal basis. Buddhist and Hindu revivalists reacted against [Christian missionary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_missionary) activities.[^cite_note-111][^cite_note-tamlwk-112] The first two decades in the 20th century are noted by the unique harmony among Sinhalese and [Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamils) political leadership, which has since been lost.[^cite_note-113]

The [1906 malaria outbreak in Ceylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1906_malaria_outbreak_in_Ceylon) actually started in the early 1900s, but the first case was documented in 1906.

In 1919, major Sinhalese and Tamil political organisations united to form the Ceylon National Congress, under the leadership of [Ponnambalam Arunachalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ponnambalam_Arunachalam),[^cite_note-114] pressing colonial masters for more constitutional reforms. But without massive popular support, and with the governor's encouragement for "communal representation" by creating a "Colombo seat" that dangled between Sinhalese and Tamils, the Congress lost momentum towards the mid-1920s.[^cite_note-115]

The [Donoughmore reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donoughmore_Constitution) of 1931 repudiated the communal representation and introduced [universal adult franchise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_suffrage) (the franchise stood at 4% before the reforms). This step was strongly criticised by the Tamil political leadership, who realised that they would be reduced to a minority in the newly created [State Council of Ceylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Council_of_Ceylon), which succeeded the legislative council.[^cite_note-tamllib-116][^cite_note-117] In 1937, Tamil leader [G. G. Ponnambalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G._G._Ponnambalam) demanded a 50–50 representation (50% for the Sinhalese and 50% for other ethnic groups) in the State Council. However, this demand was not met by the [Soulbury reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soulbury_Commission) of 1944–45.

### Contemporary history

Main article: [History of Sri Lanka (1948–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Sri_Lanka_\(1948%E2%80%93present\))

See also: [Sri Lankan independence movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_independence_movement) and [Sri Lankan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Civil_War)



The formal ceremony marking the start of self-rule, with the opening of the first parliament at [Independence Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Square,_Colombo)



J. R. Jayewardene was the most influential political figure of Sri Lanka in the 1970s and 1980s.

The Soulbury constitution ushered in [dominion status](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominion_of_Ceylon), with independence proclaimed on 4 February 1948.[^cite_note-countrst68-118] [D. S. Senanayake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Stephen_Senanayake) became the first [Prime Minister of Ceylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Sri_Lanka).[^cite_note-DS-119] Prominent [Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamils) leaders including Ponnambalam and [Arunachalam Mahadeva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arunachalam_Mahadeva) joined his cabinet.[^cite_note-tamllib-116][^cite_note-120] The [British Royal Navy](26061.md) remained stationed at [Trincomalee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trincomalee) until 1956. A countrywide [popular demonstration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1953_Ceylonese_Hartal) against withdrawal of the rice rations resulted in the resignation of prime minister [Dudley Senanayake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dudley_Senanayake).[^cite_note-islnksd-121]

[S. W. R. D. Bandaranaike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._W._R._D._Bandaranaike) was elected prime minister in 1956. His three-year rule had a profound influence through his self-proclaimed role of "defender of the besieged Sinhalese culture".[^cite_note-122] He introduced the controversial [Sinhala Only Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhala_Only_Act), recognising [Sinhala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhala_language) as the only official language of the government. Although partially reversed in 1958, the bill posed a grave concern for the Tamil community, which perceived in it a threat to their language and culture.[^cite_note-FW-123][^cite_note-AV-124][^cite_note-a1-125]

The [Federal Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illankai_Tamil_Arasu_Kachchi) (FP) launched a movement of non-violent resistance ([satyagraha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satyagraha)) against the bill, which prompted Bandaranaike to reach an agreement ([Bandaranaike–Chelvanayakam Pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandaranaike%E2%80%93Chelvanayakam_Pact)) with [S. J. V. Chelvanayakam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._J._V._Chelvanayakam), leader of the FP, to resolve the looming ethnic conflict.[^cite_note-126] The pact proved ineffective in the face of ongoing protests by opposition and the Buddhist clergy. The bill, together with various government [colonisation schemes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_state-sponsored_colonisation_schemes), contributed much towards the political rancour between Sinhalese and Tamil political leaders.[^cite_note-127] Bandaranaike was [assassinated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._W._R._D._Bandaranaike#Assassination) by an extremist Buddhist monk in 1959.[^cite_note-128]

Leaders in 1960



[Elizabeth II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_II)\
[Queen of Ceylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Ceylon_\(1948%E2%80%931972\))



[Sirimavo Bandaranaike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirimavo_Bandaranaike)\
Prime Minister

1960 saw the election of Sirimavo Bandaranaike as Ceylon's Prime Minister and the first time in world history that the heads of both state and government in a country were female.

[Sirimavo Bandaranaike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirimavo_Bandaranaike), the widow of Bandaranaike, took office as prime minister in 1960, and withstood an [attempted coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1962_Ceylonese_coup_d'%C3%A9tat_attempt) in 1962. During her second term as prime minister, the government instituted socialist economic policies, strengthening ties with the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) and China, while promoting a policy of non-alignment. In 1971, Ceylon experienced a [Marxist insurrection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1971_JVP_insurrection), which was quickly suppressed. In 1972, the country became a [republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic) named Sri Lanka, repudiating its dominion status. Prolonged minority grievances and the use of communal emotionalism as an election campaign weapon by both Sinhalese and Tamil leaders abetted a fledgling Tamil militancy in the north during the 1970s.[^cite_note-129] The [policy of standardisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Policy_of_standardisation) by the Sirimavo government to rectify disparities created in university enrolment, which was in essence an [affirmative action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affirmative_action) to assist geographically disadvantaged students to obtain tertiary education,[^cite_note-affrmact-130] resulted in reducing the proportion of Tamil students at university level and acted as the immediate catalyst for the rise of militancy.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132] The assassination of [Jaffna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaffna) [Mayor](101146.md) [Alfred Duraiyappah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Duraiyappah) in 1975 by the [Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam) (LTTE) marked a crisis point.[^cite_note-Hoffman139-133][^cite_note-rohang-134]

The government of [J. R. Jayawardene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._R._Jayawardene) swept to power in 1977, defeating the [United Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Front_\(Sri_Lanka\)) government.[^cite_note-attms-135] Jayawardene introduced a [new constitution](5308117.md), together with a [free-market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free-market_economy) and a powerful [executive presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_president) modelled after that of France. It made Sri Lanka the first [South Asian](21566765.md) country to [liberalise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberalism) its economy.[^cite_note-filbrn-136] Beginning in 1983, ethnic tensions were manifested in an [on-and-off insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_civil_war) against the government by the LTTE. An [LTTE attack on 13 soldiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_Four_Bravo) resulted in the start of a civil war, and in response anti-Tamil [race riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_July) took place, allegedly backed by [Sinhalese hard-line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhalese_Buddhist_nationalism) ministers, which resulted in more than 150,000 Tamil civilians fleeing the island, seeking asylum in other countries.[^cite_note-Remembering-137][^cite_note-lkwbblk-138]

Lapses in foreign policy resulted in India strengthening the Tigers by providing arms and training.[^cite_note-sndaytst-139][^cite_note-140][^cite_note-141] In 1987, the [Indo-Sri Lanka Accord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Sri_Lanka_Accord) was signed and the [Indian Peace Keeping Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Peace_Keeping_Force) (IPKF) was deployed in northern Sri Lanka to stabilise the region by neutralising the LTTE.[^cite_note-Stokke2000a-142] The same year, the [JVP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janatha_Vimukthi_Peramuna) launched its [second insurrection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987%E2%80%9389_JVP_Insurrection) in Southern Sri Lanka,[^cite_note-secninsr-143] necessitating redeployment of the IPKF in 1990.[^cite_note-atimes-144] In October 1990, the LTTE [expelled Sri Lankan Moors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expulsion_of_Muslims_from_the_Northern_province_by_LTTE) (Muslims by religion) from northern Sri Lanka.[^cite_note-slnp-145] In 2002, the Sri Lankan government and LTTE signed a Norwegian-mediated ceasefire agreement.[^cite_note-a1-125]

The [2004 Asian tsunami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Indian_Ocean_earthquake_and_tsunami) killed over 30,000 and displaced over 500,000 people in Sri Lanka.[^cite_note-BBC_News_2005-146]

From 1985 to 2006, the Sri Lankan government and Tamil insurgents held four rounds of peace talks without success. Both LTTE and the government resumed fighting in 2006, and the government officially backed out of the ceasefire in 2008.[^cite_note-a1-125] In 2009, under the [Presidency](359933.md) of [Mahinda Rajapaksa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahinda_Rajapaksa), the [Sri Lanka Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Armed_Forces) defeated the LTTE, bringing an end to the 26 year long civil war on 19 May 2009,[^cite_note-147][^cite_note-148][^cite_note-149][^cite_note-150] and re-established control of the entire country by the Sri Lankan Government.[^cite_note-151] Overall, between 60,000 and 100,000 people were killed during the course of the 26 year long conflict.[^cite_note-ABC200509-152][^cite_note-153]

[2019 Sri Lanka Easter bombings](60550092.md) carried out by the terrorist group [National Thowheeth Jama'ath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Thowheeth_Jama'ath) on 21 April 2019 killed 261 people.[^cite_note-timeline-154] On 26 April 2019 an anti [terrorist operation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sainthamaruthu_shootout) was carried out against the National Thowheeth Jama'ath by the [Sri Lanka Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Army) with the operation being successful and National Thowheeth Jama'ath's insurgency ending.[^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157]



[Sri Lankans protesting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Sri_Lankan_protests) in front of the [Presidential Secretariat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Secretariat_\(Sri_Lanka\)) in Colombo, 13 April 2022

Economic troubles in Sri Lanka began in 2019, when a [severe economic crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%93present_Sri_Lankan_economic_crisis) occurred caused by rapidly increasing foreign debt, massive government budget deficits due to tax cuts, falling foreign remittances, a food crisis caused by mandatory organic farming along with a ban on chemical fertilisers, and a multitude of other factors.[^cite_note-158] The Sri Lankan Government officially declared the ongoing crisis to be the worst [economic crisis in the country](27415.md) in 73 years.[^cite_note-159] In August 2021, a food emergency was declared.[^cite_note-160] In June 2022, Prime Minister [Ranil Wickremesinghe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranil_Wickremesinghe) declared the collapse of the Sri Lankan economy in parliament.[^cite_note-161] The crisis resulted in Sri Lanka defaulting on its \$51 billion sovereign debt for the first time in its history, along with double-digit inflation, a crippling energy crisis that led to approximately 15 hour power cuts, severe fuel shortages leading to the suspension of fuel to all non-essential vehicles, and more such economic disorder.[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163] Due to the crisis, massive [street protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Sri_Lankan_protests) erupted all over the country, with protesters demanding the resignation of the President [Gotabaya Rajapaksa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gotabaya_Rajapaksa). The protests culminated with the storming and siege of the [President's House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President's_House,_Colombo) on 9 July 2022, and resulted in President Gotabaya Rajapaksa fleeing to Singapore[^cite_note-164] and later emailing his resignation to parliament, formally announcing his resignation and making him the first Sri Lankan president to resign in the middle of his term.[^cite_note-165] On the same day the President's House was stormed, protesters besieged and stormed the [private residence of the prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/115_Fifth_Lane) and burnt it down.[^cite_note-166]

After [Parliament](571660.md) elected the new president as [Ranil Wickremesinghe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranil_Wickremesinghe) on [20 July 2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Sri_Lankan_presidential_election), Wickremesinghe took oath as the ninth President of Sri Lanka.[^cite_note-167] He implemented various economic reforms in efforts to stabilise Sri Lanka's economy, which has shown slight improvement since.[^cite_note-168][^cite_note-169] On 23 September 2024, [Anura Kumara Dissanayake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anura_Kumara_Dissanayake) was sworn in as Sri Lanka's new president after winning the presidential [election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Sri_Lankan_presidential_election) as a left-wing candidate.[^cite_note-170] On 14 November 2024, President Anura Kumara Dissanayake's [National People's Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_People's_Power) (NPP), a left-leaning alliance, received a two-thirds majority in parliament in Sri Lankan [parliamentary election.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Sri_Lankan_parliamentary_election)[^cite_note-171]

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Sri_Lanka)

A roughly oval island with a mountainous centre

Topographic map of Sri Lanka

Sri Lanka, an island in [South Asia](21566765.md), lies on the [Indian Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Plate), a major [tectonic plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plate_tectonics) that was formerly part of the [Indo-Australian Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Australian_Plate).[^cite_note-172] It is in the Indian Ocean southwest of the [Bay of Bengal](47892.md), between latitudes [5°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5th_parallel_north) and [10° N](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/10th_parallel_north), and longitudes [79°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/79th_meridian_east) and [82° E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/82nd_meridian_east).[^cite_note-173] Sri Lanka is separated from the mainland portion of the [Indian subcontinent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_subcontinent) by the [Gulf of Mannar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Mannar) and [Palk Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palk_Strait). According to [Hindu mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_mythology), a land bridge existed between the Indian mainland and Sri Lanka. It now amounts to only a [chain of limestone shoals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam's_Bridge) remaining above [sea level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_level).[^cite_note-174] Legends claim that it was passable on foot up to 1480 CE, until [cyclones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyclone) deepened the channel.[^cite_note-Garg1992-175][^cite_note-176] Portions are still as shallow as 1 metre (3 ft), hindering navigation.[^cite_note-177] The island consists mostly of flat to rolling coastal plains, with mountains rising only in the south-central part. The highest point is [Pidurutalagala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pidurutalagala), reaching 2,524 metres (8,281 ft) above sea level.



A view of [Sripada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam's_Peak) from [Maskeliya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maskeliya)

Sri Lanka has 103 rivers. The longest of these is the [Mahaweli River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahaweli_River), extending 335 kilometres (208 mi).[^cite_note-178] These waterways give rise to 51 natural waterfalls of 10 metres (33 ft) or more. The highest is [Bambarakanda Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bambarakanda_Falls), with a height of 263 metres (863 ft).[^cite_note-179] Sri Lanka's coastline is 1,585 km (985 mi) long.[^cite_note-unep-180] Sri Lanka claims an [exclusive economic zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone) extending 200 [nautical miles](21289.md), which is approximately 6.7 times Sri Lanka's land area. The coastline and adjacent waters support highly productive marine ecosystems such as [fringing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fringing_reef) [coral reefs](87410.md) and shallow beds of coastal and [estuarine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estuary) [seagrasses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seagrass).[^cite_note-faosl-181]

Sri Lanka has 45 [estuaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estuary) and 40 [lagoons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagoon).[^cite_note-unep-180] Sri Lanka's [mangrove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangrove) ecosystem spans over 7,000 [hectares](24919513.md) and played a vital role in buffering the force of the waves in the [2004 Indian Ocean tsunami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Indian_Ocean_earthquake_and_tsunami).[^cite_note-iucnas-182] The island is rich in minerals such as [ilmenite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilmenite), [feldspar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feldspar), [graphite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graphite), [silica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silica), [kaolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaolin), [mica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mica) and [thorium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thorium).[^cite_note-minr-183][^cite_note-thor-184] Existence of [petroleum](23195.md) and gas in the Gulf of Mannar has also been confirmed, and the extraction of recoverable quantities is underway.[^cite_note-prdssl-185]

### Climate

See also: [Geography of Sri Lanka § Climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Sri_Lanka#Climate)



Sri Lanka map of Köppen climate classification

The climate is [tropical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_climate) and warm because of moderating effects of ocean winds. Mean temperatures range from 17 °C (62.6 °F) in the [Central Highlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Highlands_of_Sri_Lanka), where frost may occur for several days in the winter, to a maximum of 33 °C (91.4 °F) in low-altitude areas. Average yearly temperatures range from 28 °C (82.4 °F) to nearly 31 °C (87.8 °F). Day and night temperatures may vary by 14 °C (25 °F) to 18 °C (32 °F).[^cite_note-186]

The rainfall pattern is influenced by [monsoon](57630.md) winds from the Indian Ocean and Bay of Bengal. The "wet zone" and some of the windward slopes of the central highlands receive up to 2,500 millimetres (98.4 in) of rain each year, but the leeward slopes in the east and northeast receive little rain. Most of the east, southeast, and northern parts of Sri Lanka constitute the "dry zone", which receives between 1,200 and 1,900 mm (47 and 75 in) of rain annually.[^cite_note-187]

The arid northwest and southeast coasts receive the least rain at 800 to 1,200 mm (31 to 47 in) per year. Periodic squalls occur and sometimes [tropical cyclones](8282374.md) bring overcast skies and rains to the southwest, northeast, and eastern parts of the island. Humidity is typically higher in the southwest and mountainous areas and depends on the seasonal patterns of rainfall.[^cite_note-188] An increase in average rainfall coupled with heavier rainfall events has resulted in recurrent flooding and related damages to infrastructure, utility supply and the urban economy.[^cite_note-189]

### Flora and fauna

Main articles: [Environment of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environment_of_Sri_Lanka) and [Wildlife of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_of_Sri_Lanka)

See also: [List of mammals of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mammals_of_Sri_Lanka) and [List of birds of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_birds_of_Sri_Lanka)



The [Sri Lankan elephant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_elephant) is one of three recognised [subspecies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subspecies) of the [Asian elephant](379035.md). The 2011 elephant census estimated a population of 5,879.

[^cite_note-190]

The [Western Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Ghats) of India and Sri Lanka were included among the first 18 global [biodiversity hotspots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity_hotspot) due to high levels of species endemism. The number of biodiversity hotspots has now increased to 34.[^cite_note-Current_Science-191] Sri Lanka has the highest biodiversity per unit area among Asian countries for flowering plants and all vertebrate groups except birds.[^cite_note-mahindapala-192] A remarkably high proportion of the species among its flora and fauna, 27% of the 3,210 flowering plants and 22% of the mammals, are [endemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemism).[^cite_note-193] Sri Lanka supports a rich avifauna of that stands at 453 species and this includes 240 species of birds that are known to breed in the country. [33 species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_endemic_birds_of_Sri_Lanka) are accepted by some ornithologists as endemic while some ornithologists consider only 27 are endemic and the remaining six are considered as proposed endemics.[^cite_note-redlist2012-194] Sri Lanka's protected areas are administered by two government bodies: The [Department of Forest Conservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Forest_Conservation_\(Sri_Lanka\)) and the [Department of Wildlife Conservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Wildlife_Conservation_\(Sri_Lanka\)). Department of Wildlife Conservation administers 61 wildlife sanctuaries, 22 national parks, four nature reserves, three strict nature reserves, and one jungle corridor; while Department of Forest Conservation oversees 65 conservation forests and one national heritage wilderness area. 26.5% of the country's land area is legally protected. This is a higher percentage of protected areas when compared to the rest of Asia.[^cite_note-195]

Sri Lanka contains four terrestrial ecoregions: [Sri Lanka lowland rain forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_lowland_rain_forests), [Sri Lanka montane rain forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_montane_rain_forests), [Sri Lanka dry-zone dry evergreen forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_dry-zone_dry_evergreen_forests), and [Deccan thorn scrub forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_thorn_scrub_forests).[^cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-196] Flowering [acacias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acacia) flourish on the arid [Jaffna Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaffna_Peninsula). Among the trees of the dry-land forests are valuable species such as [satinwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chloroxylon), [ebony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebony), [ironwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesua_ferrea), [mahogany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahogany) and [teak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teak). The wet zone is a tropical evergreen forest with tall trees, broad foliage, and a dense undergrowth of vines and creepers. Subtropical evergreen forests resembling those of temperate climates flourish in the higher altitudes.[^cite_note-197]



The [Sri Lankan leopard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_leopard) (*Panthera pardus kotiya*) is an [endangered](20110668.md) subspecies of [leopard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopard) native to Sri Lanka.

[Yala National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yala_National_Park) in the southeast protects herds of elephant, deer, and peacocks. The [Wilpattu National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilpattu_National_Park) in the northwest, the largest national park, preserves the habitats of many water birds such as storks, pelicans, ibis, and spoonbills. The island has four [biosphere reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature_reserve): [Bundala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bundala_National_Park), [Hurulu Forest Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurulu_Forest_Reserve), the [Kanneliya-Dediyagala-Nakiyadeniya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanneliya-Dediyagala-Nakiyadeniya), and [Sinharaja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinharaja_Forest_Reserve).[^cite_note-unesco_biosphere_reserves-198] Sinharaja is home to 26 endemic birds and 20 rainforest species, including the elusive [red-faced malkoha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red-faced_malkoha), the [green-billed coucal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green-billed_coucal) and the [Sri Lanka blue magpie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_blue_magpie). The untapped genetic potential of Sinharaja flora is enormous. Of the 211 woody trees and lianas within the reserve, 139 (66%) are endemic. The total vegetation density, including trees, shrubs, herbs, and seedlings, has been estimated at 240,000 individuals per hectare. The Minneriya National Park borders the [Minneriya Tank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minneriya_Tank), which is an important source of water for elephants inhabiting the surrounding forests. Dubbed "The Gathering", the congregation of elephants can be seen on the tank-bed in the late dry season (August to October) as the surrounding water sources steadily disappear. The park also encompasses a range of micro-habitats which include classic dry zone tropical monsoonal evergreen forest, thick stands of giant bamboo, hilly pastures (patanas), and grasslands (talawas).[^cite_note-Minneriya-199]



Maha rath mala (*Rhododendron arboreum* ssp. *zeylanicum*) is a rare sub-species of *[Rhododendron arboreum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhododendron_arboreum)* found in [Central Highlands of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Highlands_of_Sri_Lanka).

During the Mahaweli Programme of the 1970s and 1980s in northern Sri Lanka, the government set aside four areas of land totalling 1,900 km<sup>2</sup> (730 sq mi) as national parks. Statistics of Sri Lanka's forest cover show rapid deforestation from 1956 to 2010. In 1956, 44.2 percent of the country's land area had forest cover. Forest cover depleted rapidly in recent decades; 29.6 percent in 1999, 28.7 percent in 2010.[^cite_note-forests-200]

## Government and politics

Main articles: [Government of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Sri_Lanka) and [Politics of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Sri_Lanka)

See also: [Constitution of Sri Lanka](5308117.md)



The [old Sri Lankan Parliament building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Parliament_Building,_Colombo), near the [Galle Face Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galle_Face_Green) in Colombo. It now serves as the [Presidential Secretariat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Secretariat_\(Sri_Lanka\))'s headquarters.

Sri Lanka is a [democratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy) [republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic) and a [unitary state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state) which is governed by a [semi-presidential system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-presidential_system).[^cite_note-constnt-201] Sri Lanka is the oldest democracy in Asia.[^cite_note-202] Most provisions of the constitution can be amended by a [two-thirds majority](638313.md) in [parliament](571660.md). The amendment of [certain fundamental features](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entrenched_clause), including clauses on national symbols, religion, term limits, the reference to Sri Lanka as a unitary state, and the entrenchment mechanism itself, requires both a two-thirds majority in Parliament and approval in a nationwide [referendum](51582.md).[^cite_note-\:0-203][^cite_note-204] The [Constitution of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_Democratic_Socialist_Republic_of_Sri_Lanka) officially declares it to be a [socialist state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_state).[^cite_note-205]

In common with many democracies, the Sri Lankan government has three branches:

- **Executive**: The [President of Sri Lanka](359933.md) is the [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state); the [commander in chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief) of the armed forces; [chief executive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_executive_\(gubernatorial\)), and is popularly elected for a five-year term.

  [^cite_note-206]

  &#x20;The president heads the [cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_\(government\)) and appoints [ministers](739237.md) from elected [members of parliament](98066.md).

  [^cite_note-207]

  &#x20;The president is immune from legal proceedings while in the office with respect to any acts done or omitted to be done by him or her in either an official or private capacity.

  [^cite_note-208]

  &#x20;Following the passage of the [19th amendment to the constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nineteenth_Amendment_to_the_Constitution_of_Sri_Lanka) in 2015, the president has two terms, which previously stood at no term limit.

- **Legislative**: The [Parliament of Sri Lanka](571660.md) is a [unicameral](204304.md) 225-member legislature with 196 members elected from 22 multi-seat constituencies and 29 elected by [proportional representation](23298.md).

  [^cite_note-209]

  &#x20;Members are elected by universal suffrage for a five-year term. The president may summon, suspend, or end a legislative session and dissolve Parliament anytime after four and a half years. The parliament reserves the power to make all laws.

  [^cite_note-parliam-210]

  &#x20;The president's deputy and [head of government](37570.md), the [prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Sri_Lanka), leads the [ruling party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruling_party) in parliament and shares many executive responsibilities, mainly in domestic affairs.



The Supreme Court of Sri Lanka, Colombo

- **Judicial**: Sri Lanka's judiciary consists of a [Supreme Court](2830384.md)—the highest and final superior court of record,

  [^cite_note-parliam-210]

  &#x20;a Court of Appeal, High Courts, and a number of subordinate courts. The highly complex legal system reflects diverse cultural influences.

  [^cite_note-unpanh-211]

  &#x20;Criminal law is based almost entirely on [British law](85441.md). Basic civil law derives from [Roman-Dutch law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman-Dutch_law). Laws pertaining to marriage, divorce, and inheritance are [communal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_law).

  [^cite_note-lawsd-212]

  &#x20;Because of ancient customary practices and religion, the Sinhala customary law (Kandyan law), the [Thesavalamai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thesavalamai), and [Sharia](28840.md) law are followed in special cases.

  [^cite_note-custmryl-213]

  &#x20;The president appoints judges to the Supreme Court, the Court of Appeal, and the High Courts. A judicial service commission, composed of the [chief justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Justice_of_Sri_Lanka) and two Supreme Court judges, appoints, transfers, and dismisses lower court judges.

### Politics

Main article: [Politics of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Sri_Lanka)

See also: [Elections in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Sri_Lanka)

| [Sri Lanka](26750.md) [National symbols of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_symbols_of_Sri_Lanka) |  |

| [v](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Sri_Lankan_symbols)[t](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template_talk\:Sri_Lankan_symbols)[e](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Template\:Sri_Lankan_symbols) |  |

The current political culture in Sri Lanka is a contest between two rival coalitions led by the [centre-left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre-left_politics) and [progressive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressivism) [United People's Freedom Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_People's_Freedom_Alliance) (UPFA), an offspring of [Sri Lanka Freedom Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Freedom_Party) (SLFP), and the comparatively [right-wing](56522.md) and pro-[capitalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitalism) [United National Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_National_Party) (UNP). After 2018, two major political parties split from these two parties: The [Samagi Jana Balawegaya](63089955.md) split from the UNP, and the [Sri Lanka Podujana Peramuna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Podujana_Peramuna) split from the UPFA. The third wing party [Janatha Vimukthi Peramuna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janatha_Vimukthi_Peramuna) has gained popularity since 2022.[^cite_note-216]

Sri Lanka is essentially a multi-party democracy with many smaller Buddhist, socialist, and Tamil nationalist political parties. As of July 2011, the number of registered political parties in the country is 67.[^cite_note-217] Of these, the [Lanka Sama Samaja Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lanka_Sama_Samaja_Party) (LSSP), established in 1935, is the oldest.[^cite_note-218]

The UNP, established by D. S. Senanayake in 1946, was until recently the largest single political party.[^cite_note-unpl-219] It is the only political group which had representation in all parliaments since independence.[^cite_note-unpl-219] SLFP was founded by [S. W. R. D. Bandaranaike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._W._R._D._Bandaranaike) in July 1951.[^cite_note-slfps-220] SLFP registered its first victory in 1956, defeating the ruling UNP in the [1956 Parliamentary election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1956_Ceylonese_parliamentary_election).[^cite_note-slfps-220] Following the parliamentary election in [July 1960](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/July_1960_Ceylonese_parliamentary_election), [Sirimavo Bandaranaike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirimavo_Bandaranaike) became the prime minister and the world's first elected [female head of government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_elected_and_appointed_female_heads_of_state_and_government).[^cite_note-bbcsirimavo-221]

[G. G. Ponnambalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G._G._Ponnambalam), the [Tamil nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Tamil_nationalism) counterpart of S. W. R. D. Bandaranaike,[^cite_note-socs-222] founded the [All Ceylon Tamil Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_Ceylon_Tamil_Congress) (ACTC) in 1944. Objecting to Ponnambalam's cooperation with D. S. Senanayake, a dissident group led by [S.J.V. Chelvanayakam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._J._V._Chelvanayakam) broke away in 1949 and formed the [Illankai Tamil Arasu Kachchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illankai_Tamil_Arasu_Kachchi) (ITAK), also known as the Federal Party, becoming the main Tamil political party in Sri Lanka for the next two decades.[^cite_note-poltitak-223] The Federal Party advocated a more aggressive stance toward the Sinhalese.[^cite_note-postcolhist-224] With the constitutional reforms of 1972, the ACTC and ITAK created the Tamil United Front (later [Tamil United Liberation Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_United_Liberation_Front)). Following a period of turbulence as Tamil militants rose to power in the late 1970s, these Tamil political parties were succeeded in October 2001 by the [Tamil National Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_National_Alliance).[^cite_note-postcolhist-224][^cite_note-tnas-225] [Janatha Vimukthi Peramuna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janatha_Vimukthi_Peramuna), a [Marxist–Leninist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism%E2%80%93Leninism) political party founded by [Rohana Wijeweera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rohana_Wijeweera) in 1965, serves as a third force in the current political context.[^cite_note-jvpnew-226] It endorses leftist policies which are more radical than the traditionalist leftist politics of the LSSP and the [Communist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_Sri_Lanka).[^cite_note-postcolhist-224] Founded in 1981, the [Sri Lanka Muslim Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Muslim_Congress) is the largest Muslim political party in Sri Lanka.[^cite_note-muslcon-227]

President [Mahinda Rajapaksa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahinda_Rajapaksa) lost the [2015 presidential elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Sri_Lankan_presidential_election), ending his ten-year presidency. However, his successor as Sri Lankan President, [Maithripala Sirisena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maithripala_Sirisena), decided not to seek re-election in 2019.[^cite_note-228] The Rajapaksa family regained power in November [2019 presidential elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Sri_Lankan_presidential_election) when Mahinda's younger brother and former wartime defence chief [Gotabaya Rajapaksa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gotabaya_Rajapaksa) won the election, and he was later sworn in as the new president of Sri Lanka.[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230] Their firm grip of power was consolidated in the [parliamentary elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Sri_Lankan_parliamentary_election) in August 2020. The family's political party, [Sri Lanka People's Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_People's_Front) (known by its Sinhala initials SLPP), obtained a landslide victory and a clear majority in the parliament. Five members of the Rajapaksa family won seats in the new parliament. Former president Mahinda Rajapaksa became the new prime minister.[^cite_note-231]

In 2022, a [political crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Sri_Lankan_political_crisis) started due to the power struggle between [President](359933.md) [Gotabaya Rajapaksa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gotabaya_Rajapaksa) and the [Parliament of Sri Lanka](571660.md). The crisis was fuelled by [anti-government protests and demonstrations by the public](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Sri_Lankan_protests) and also due to the worsening [economy of Sri Lanka](27415.md) since 2019. The anti-government sentiment across various parts of Sri Lanka has triggered unprecedented political instability, creating shockwaves in the political arena.[^cite_note-232]

On 20 July 2022, [Ranil Wickremesinghe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranil_Wickremesinghe) was elected as the ninth President via a [parliamentarian election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Sri_Lankan_presidential_election).[^cite_note-233] President Ranil Wickremesinghe was defeated by left-leaning [Anura Kumara Dissanayake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anura_Kumara_Dissanayake) in 2024 presidential [elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Sri_Lankan_presidential_election).[^cite_note-234]

### Administrative divisions

Main article: [Administrative divisions of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Sri_Lanka)

For administrative purposes, Sri Lanka is divided into nine [provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_Sri_Lanka)[^cite_note-235] and twenty-five [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Sri_Lanka).[^cite_note-236]

#### Provinces

Provinces in Sri Lanka have existed since the 19th century, but they had no legal status until 1987 when the 13th Amendment of the 1978 constitution established provincial councils after several decades of increasing demand for a [decentralisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devolution) of the government.[^cite_note-official-237] Each provincial council is an autonomous body not under the authority of any ministry. Some of its functions had been undertaken by central government ministries, departments, corporations, and statutory authorities,[^cite_note-official-237] but authority over land and police is not as a rule given to provincial councils.[^cite_note-landpolc-238][^cite_note-landpolc2-239] Between 1989 and 2006, the Northern and Eastern provinces were temporarily merged to form the [North-East Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Eastern_Province,_Sri_Lanka).[^cite_note-lnpx-240][^cite_note-norteastn-241] Prior to 1987, all administrative tasks for the provinces were handled by a district-based civil service which had been in place since colonial times. Now each province is administered by a directly elected provincial council:
