#### Districts and local authorities

Each district is administered under a [district secretariat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_Agent_\(Sri_Lanka\)). The districts are further subdivided into 256 [divisional secretariats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divisional_Secretariats_of_Sri_Lanka), and these to approximately 14,008 [Grama Niladhari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grama_Niladhari) divisions.[^cite_note-tstasgov-245] The districts are known in Sinhala as *disa* and in Tamil as *māwaddam*. Originally, a *disa* (usually rendered into English as Dissavony) was a [duchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duchy), notably Matale and Uva.

There are three other types of local authorities: municipal councils (18), urban councils (13) and pradeshiya sabha, also called pradesha sabhai (256).[^cite_note-246] Local authorities were originally based on feudal counties named *korale* and *rata*, and were formerly known as "D.R.O. divisions" after the divisional revenue officer.[^cite_note-247] Later, the D.R.O.s became "assistant government agents", and the divisions were known as "A.G.A. divisions". These divisional secretariats are currently administered by a divisional secretary.

### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Sri_Lanka)

See also: [Sri Lanka and the Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_and_the_Non-Aligned_Movement)



President [J. R. Jayewardene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._R._Jayewardene) gifting [Jayathu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jayathu), a baby elephant to US President [Ronald Reagan](25433.md) in 1984



Sri Lankan Prime Minister [Mahinda Rajapaksa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahinda_Rajapaksa) with Indian Prime Minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) in 2020

Sri Lanka is a founding member of the [Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement) (NAM). While ensuring that it maintains its independence, Sri Lanka has cultivated [relations with India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India%E2%80%93Sri_Lanka_relations).[^cite_note-relatns-248] Sri Lanka became a member of the [United Nations](31769.md) in 1955. Today, it is also a member of the [Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations), the [SAARC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asian_Association_for_Regional_Cooperation), the [World Bank](36752.md), the [International Monetary Fund](15251.md), the [Asian Development Bank](2512.md), and the [Colombo Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombo_Plan).

The United National Party has traditionally favoured links with the West, while the Sri Lanka Freedom Party has favoured links with the East.[^cite_note-relatns-248] Sri Lankan Finance Minister J. R. Jayewardene, together with then Australian Foreign Minister Sir Percy Spencer, proposed the Colombo Plan at the Commonwealth Foreign Minister's Conference held in Colombo in 1950.[^cite_note-249] At the [San Francisco Peace Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_San_Francisco) in 1951, while many countries were reluctant, Sri Lanka argued for a free [Japan](15573.md) and refused to accept payment of reparations for [World War II](32927.md) damage because it believed it would harm Japan's economy.[^cite_note-250] Sri Lanka-China relations started as soon as the People's Republic of China was formed in 1949. The two countries signed an important [Rubber-Rice Pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceylon-China_Trade_Agreement_of_1952) in 1952.[^cite_note-251] Sri Lanka played a vital role at the [Asian–African Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian%E2%80%93African_Conference) in 1955, which was an important step in the crystallisation of the NAM.[^cite_note-252]

The Bandaranaike government of 1956 significantly changed the pro-western policies set by the previous UNP government. It recognised Cuba under [Fidel Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidel_Castro) in 1959. Shortly afterward, Cuba's revolutionary [Che Guevara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Che_Guevara) paid a visit to Sri Lanka.[^cite_note-253] The [Sirima-Shastri Pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirima%E2%80%93Shastri_Pact) of 1964[^cite_note-254] and [Sirima-Gandhi Pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirima%E2%80%93Gandhi_Pact) of 1974[^cite_note-255] were signed between Sri Lankan and Indian leaders in an attempt to solve the [long-standing dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceylon_Citizenship_Act) over the status of [plantation workers of Indian origin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Tamils_of_Sri_Lanka). In 1974, [Kachchatheevu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katchatheevu), a small island in [Palk Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palk_Strait), was formally ceded to Sri Lanka.[^cite_note-256] By this time, Sri Lanka was strongly involved in the NAM, and the [fifth NAM summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5th_Summit_of_the_Non-Aligned_Movement) was held in Colombo in 1976.[^cite_note-257] The relationship between Sri Lanka and India became tense under the government of [J. R. Jayawardene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junius_Richard_Jayewardene).[^cite_note-atimes-144][^cite_note-indnrel-258] To this end, Sri Lanka had briefly adopted a 'Look [East](28741.md)' policy,[^cite_note-\:8-259]<sup>: 1 </sup> and at one point pursued [ASEAN membership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accession_of_Sri_Lanka_to_ASEAN) over joining SAARC.[^cite_note-\:72-260][^cite_note-\:5-261]<sup>: 64 </sup> Further deterioration led to the [Indian intervention in the Sri Lankan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_intervention_in_the_Sri_Lankan_Civil_War) and subsequently deployed an [Indian Peace Keeping Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Peace_Keeping_Force) in 1987.[^cite_note-262] In the present, Sri Lanka enjoys extensive relations with China,[^cite_note-263] Russia,[^cite_note-264] and Pakistan.[^cite_note-265]

### Military

Main article: [Sri Lanka Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Armed_Forces)

The Sri Lanka Armed Forces, comprising the [Sri Lanka Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Army), the [Sri Lanka Navy](6186841.md), and the [Sri Lanka Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Air_Force), come under the purview of the [Ministry of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_\(Sri_Lanka\)).[^cite_note-ReferenceA-266] The total strength of the three services is about 346,000 personnel, with nearly 36,000 reserves.[^cite_note-267] Sri Lanka has not enforced [military conscription](5735.md).[^cite_note-268] Paramilitary units include the [Special Task Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Task_Force_\(Sri_Lanka\)), the [Civil Security Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Civil_Security_Force), and the [Sri Lanka Coast Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Coast_Guard).[^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270]

Since independence in 1948, the primary focus of the armed forces has been internal security, crushing three major insurgencies, two by [Marxist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism) militants of the [JVP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janatha_Vimukthi_Peramuna) and a 26-year-long conflict with the [LTTE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam). The armed forces have been in a continuous mobilised state for the last 30 years.[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272] The Sri Lankan Armed Forces have engaged in [United Nations](31769.md) [peacekeeping](84617.md) operations since the early 1960s, contributing forces to permanent contingents deployed in several UN peacekeeping missions in [Chad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Mission_in_the_Central_African_Republic_and_Chad), [Lebanon](787766.md), and [Haiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Stabilisation_Mission_in_Haiti).[^cite_note-unmissions09-273]

Sri Lanka is the 100th most peaceful country in the world, according to the 2024 [Global Peace Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Peace_Index).[^cite_note-274]

### Human rights

Main article: [Human rights in Sri Lanka](4139272.md)

Officially, the constitution of Sri Lanka guarantees human rights as ratified by the United Nations. However, several groups, such as [Amnesty International](18947898.md), [Freedom from Torture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_from_Torture), [Human Rights Watch](46530.md),[^cite_note-275] as well as the [British government](25318118.md)[^cite_note-276] and the [United States Department of State](31975.md) have criticised human rights violations in Sri Lanka.[^cite_note-277] The [Sri Lankan Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Sri_Lanka) and the LTTE have both been accused of violating human rights. A [report by an advisory panel to the UN secretary-general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Report_of_the_Secretary-General's_Panel_of_Experts_on_Accountability_in_Sri_Lanka) accused both the LTTE and the Sri Lankan government of [war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alleged_war_crimes_during_the_Sri_Lankan_Civil_War) during the final stages of the civil war.[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279] Corruption remains a problem in Sri Lanka, and there is little protection for those who stand up against corruption.[^cite_note-280] The 135-year-old [Article 365 of the Sri Lankan Penal Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_365_of_the_Sri_Lankan_Penal_Code) criminalises [homosexual acts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Sri_Lanka), with a penalty of up to ten years in prison.[^cite_note-281]

The [UN Human Rights Council](635790.md) has documented [over 12,000 named individuals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enforced_disappearances_in_Sri_Lanka) who have disappeared after detention by security forces in Sri Lanka, the second-highest figure in the world since the Working Group came into being in 1980.[^cite_note-UNHRC_involuntary_disappearances_report-282] The Sri Lankan government confirmed that 6,445 of these died. Allegations of human rights abuses have not ended with the close of the ethnic conflict.[^cite_note-People_are_disappearing-283]

[UN Human Rights Commissioner](22463.md) [Navanethem Pillay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navanethem_Pillay) visited Sri Lanka in May 2013. After her visit, she said: "The war may have ended \[in Sri Lanka], but in the meantime, democracy has been undermined and the rule of law eroded." Pillay spoke about the military's increasing involvement in civilian life and reports of military [land grabbing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_grabbing). She also said that, while in Sri Lanka, she had been allowed to go wherever she wanted, but that Sri Lankans who came to meet her were harassed and intimidated by security forces.[^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285]

In 2012, the UK charity [Freedom from Torture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_from_Torture) reported that it had received 233 referrals of torture survivors from Sri Lanka for clinical treatment or other services provided by the charity. In the same year, the group published *Out of the Silence*, which documents evidence of torture in Sri Lanka and demonstrates that the practice has continued long after the end of the civil war in 2009.[^cite_note-286] On 29 July 2020, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) said that the Sri Lanka government has targeted lawyers, human rights defenders, and journalists to suppress criticism against the government.[^cite_note-287]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Sri Lanka](27415.md)

See also: [Agriculture in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Sri_Lanka), [Tea production in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea_production_in_Sri_Lanka), [Tourism in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Sri_Lanka), [Transport in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Sri_Lanka), and [Nationalisation in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalisation_in_Sri_Lanka)



Development of real GDP per capita, 1820 to 2018

According to the International Monetary Fund, Sri Lanka's [GDP](12594.md) in terms of [purchasing power parity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity) is the second highest in the South Asian region in terms of [per capita income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Per_capita_income). In the 19th and 20th centuries, Sri Lanka became a [plantation economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plantation_economy) famous for its production and export of [cinnamon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinnamon), [rubber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubber), and [Ceylon tea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea_production_in_Sri_Lanka), which remains a trademark national export.[^cite_note-plnyn-288] The [development of modern ports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Ports_and_Aviation_\(Sri_Lanka\)) under British rule raised the strategic importance of the island as a centre of trade.[^cite_note-startim-289] From 1948 to 1977, socialism strongly influenced the government's economic policies. Colonial plantations were dismantled, [industries were nationalised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalisation_in_Sri_Lanka), and a [welfare state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare_state) established. In 1977, the [free market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_market) economy was introduced to the country, incorporating privatisation, deregulation, and the promotion of private enterprise.[^cite_note-filbrn-136]



The [World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_Colombo) in Colombo. [Presidential Secretariat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Secretariat_\(Sri_Lanka\)), Bank of Ceylon and Galadari Hotel are also visible in the image.

While the production and export of tea, rubber, coffee, sugar, and other commodities remain important, industrialisation has increased the importance of food processing, textiles, telecommunications, and finance. The country's main economic sectors are tourism, tea export, clothing, rice production, and other agricultural products. In addition to these economic sectors, overseas employment, especially in the Middle East, contributes substantially in foreign exchange.[^cite_note-treasry-290]

As of 2020, the service sector makes up 59.7% of GDP, the industrial sector 26.2%, and the agriculture sector 8.4%.[^cite_note-Annual_Report_2020-291] The private sector accounts for 85% of the economy.[^cite_note-adbrep-292] China, India and the United States are Sri Lanka's largest trading partners.[^cite_note-Sri_Lanka's_Trading_Partners-293] Economic disparities exist between the provinces with the Western Province contributing 45.1% of the GDP and the Southern Province and the Central Province contributing 10.7% and 10%, respectively.[^cite_note-ecdisp-294] With the end of the war, the Northern Province reported a record 22.9% GDP growth in 2010.[^cite_note-northpr-295]



Sri Lanka's most widely known export, [Ceylon tea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceylon_tea), which [ISO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISO) considers the cleanest tea in the world in terms of pesticide residues. Sri Lanka is also the world's 3rd largest exporter of tea.

[^cite_note-clnst-296]

The per capita income of Sri Lanka doubled from 2005 to 2011.[^cite_note-toplsl-297] During the same period, poverty dropped from 15.2% to 7.6%, unemployment rate dropped from 7.2% to 4.9%, [market capitalisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_capitalisation) of the [Colombo Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombo_Stock_Exchange) quadrupled, and the [budget deficit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_budget_deficit) doubled.[^cite_note-treasry-290] 99% of the households in Sri Lanka are electrified; 93.2% of the population have access to safe drinking water; and 53.1% have access to pipe-borne water.[^cite_note-Annual_Report_2020-291] Income inequality has also dropped in recent years, indicated by a [Gini coefficient](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gini_coefficient) of 0.36 in 2010.[^cite_note-statgovineq-298]

In 2016, Sri Lanka ranked 5th in the [World Giving Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Giving_Index), registering high levels of contentment and charitable behaviour in its society.[^cite_note-299] In 2010, *[The New York Times](30680.md)* placed Sri Lanka at the top of its list of 31 places to visit.[^cite_note-300] [S\&P Dow Jones Indices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S\&P_Dow_Jones_Indices) classifies Sri Lanka as a [frontier market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frontier_markets) as of 2018.[^cite_note-301] [Sri Lanka ranks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_Human_Development_Index) well above other South Asian countries in the [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index) (HDI) with an index of 0.750.



Sri Lanka's commercial capital [Colombo city](56636.md) at night, in 2024

By 2016, the country's debt soared as it was developing its infrastructure to the point of near bankruptcy which required a bailout from the [International Monetary Fund](15251.md) (IMF).[^cite_note-302] The IMF had agreed to provide a US\$1.5 billion bailout loan in April 2016 after Sri Lanka provided a set of criteria intended to improve its economy. By the fourth quarter of 2016, the debt was estimated to be \$64.9 billion. Additional debt had been incurred in the past by state-owned organisations and this was said to be at least \$9.5 billion. Since early 2015, domestic debt increased by 12% and external debt by 25%.[^cite_note-303] In November 2016, the IMF reported that the initial disbursement was larger than US\$150 million originally planned, a full US\$162.6 million (SDR 119.894 million). The agency's evaluation for the first tranche was cautiously optimistic about the future. Under the programme, the Sri Lankan government implemented a new Inland Revenue Act and an automatic fuel pricing formula which was noted by the IMF in its fourth review. In 2018 China agreed to bail out Sri Lanka with a loan of \$1.25 billion to deal with foreign debt repayment spikes in 2019 to 2021.[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306]

In September 2021, Sri Lanka declared a major [economic crisis](2878852.md).[^cite_note-BBC_News_2021_crisis-307] The Chief of its Central Bank has stepped down amid the crisis.[^cite_note-Bloomberg_2021-308] The Parliament has declared emergency regulations due to the crisis, seeking to ban "food hoarding".[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera_2021_crisis-309][^cite_note-310]

Tourism, which provided the economy with an input of foreign currency, significantly declined as a result of the COVID-19 pandemic.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times_2021sri-311] Nevertheless, tourism has been able to gain momentum once again post-COVID, with over 1.3 million tourist arrivals halfway through 2026.[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313]

### Transport

Main article: [Transport in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Sri_Lanka)



[Colombo Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Colombo) is the largest and busiest port in Sri Lanka

Sri Lanka has an extensive road network for inland transportation. With more than 100,000 km (62,000 mi) of paved roads,[^cite_note-314] it has one of the highest road densities in the world (1.5 km or 0.93 mi of paved roads per every 1 km<sup>2</sup> or 0.39 sq mi of land). The road network consists of 35 [A-Grade highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_A-Grade_highways_in_Sri_Lanka) and [four](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expressways_of_Sri_Lanka) [controlled-access highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controlled-access_highway).[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316] A and B grade roads are national (arterial) highways administered by Road Development Authority.[^cite_note-317] C and D grade roads are provincial roads coming under the purview of the Provincial Road Development Authority of the respective province. The other roads are local roads falling under local government authorities.

The railway network, operated by the state-run National Railway operator [Sri Lanka Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Railways), spans 1,447 kilometres (900 mi).[^cite_note-318] Sri Lanka also has three [deep-water ports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep-water_port) at [Colombo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Colombo), [Galle](426566.md), and [Trincomalee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trincomalee), in addition to the newest port being built at [Hambantota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruhunu_Magampura_International_Port).

### Transition to biological agriculture

In June 2021, Sri Lanka imposed a nationwide ban on inorganic fertilisers and pesticides. The programme was welcomed by its adviser [Vandana Shiva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vandana_Shiva),[^cite_note-319] but ignored critical voices from scientific and farming community who warned about possible collapse of farming,[^cite_note-\:7-320][^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322][^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324] including financial crisis due to devaluation of national currency pivoted around tea industry.[^cite_note-\:7-320] The situation in the tea industry was described as critical, with farming under the organic programme being described as ten times more expensive and producing half of the yield by the farmers.[^cite_note-325] In September 2021 the government declared an economic emergency, as the situation was further aggravated by falling national currency exchange rate, inflation rising as a result of high food prices, and pandemic restrictions in tourism which further decreased country's income.[^cite_note-BBC_News_2021_crisis-307]

In November 2021, Sri Lanka abandoned its plan to become the world's first organic farming nation following rising food prices and weeks of protests against the plan.[^cite_note-326] As of December 2021, the damage to agricultural production was already done, with prices having risen substantially for vegetables in Sri Lanka, and time needed to recover from the crisis. The ban on fertiliser has been lifted for certain crops, but the price of urea has risen internationally due to the price for oil and gas.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times_2021sri-311] Jeevika Weerahewa, a senior lecturer at the [University of Peradeniya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Peradeniya), predicted that the ban would reduce the paddy harvest in 2022 by an unprecedented 50%.[^cite_note-327]

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Sri_Lanka)

Sri Lanka's tourism industry is recovering, with tourism revenue reaching over \$1.5 billion in the first half of 2024, a 78% increase year-on-year. Tourist arrivals also increased to 1.01 million, up 62% from the same period in 2023. This growth is attributed to the government's proactive measures and improvements from [SriLankan Airlines](483603.md).[^cite_note-k997-328] The government launched a global tourism marketing campaign and offered visa-free entry for visitors from several countries to encourage tourism. A free tourist visa programme was introduced for visitors from selected countries, allowing them to stay for up to 30 days.[^cite_note-k997-328]

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Sri_Lanka)



Sri Lanka's population, 1871–2001

Sri Lanka had a population of 21,781,800 at the 2024 census. The average annual population growth rate between the 2012 and 2024 censuses was 0.5%. Population density was highest in [Colombo District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombo_District), at 3,551 people per square kilometre, while the [Western Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Province,_Sri_Lanka) accounted for 28.1% of the country's population.[^cite_note-SL2024CensusSummary-329] Provisional figures for 2024 put the [crude birth rate](515898.md) at 10.1 births per 1,000 people and the [crude death rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mortality_rate) at 7.8 deaths per 1,000 people.[^cite_note-330]

[Sinhalese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhalese_people) constituted the largest ethnic group in the 2024 census, accounting for 74.12% of the population. [Sri Lankan Tamils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Tamils) accounted for 12.31%, [Sri Lankan Moors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Moors)—recorded in the census as Sri Lanka Moor/Muslim—for 10.47%, and [Indian Tamils/Malayaga Thamilar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Tamils_of_Sri_Lanka) for 2.76%. Smaller communities include [Burghers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burgher_people), [Malays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Malays), [Sri Lankan Chetties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Chetties), [Bharathas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharatha_people) and [Veddas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vedda_people).[^cite_note-SL2024Census-331] Burghers trace their ancestry to European settlers, while the Malay community has roots in Southeast Asia.[^cite_note-332] The Veddas are an indigenous people of the island.[^cite_note-333]

### Languages

Main article: [Languages of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Sri_Lanka)

[Sinhala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhala_language) and [Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language) are Sri Lanka's [official languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_language) and national languages. The Constitution designates [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_English) as the link language.[^cite_note-SLConstitutionDemographics-336] English is widely used in education, science and commerce. Some members of the [Burgher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burgher_people) community, particularly on the east coast, speak [Portuguese Creole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Portuguese_creole). Dutch, formerly spoken within the community, fell into disuse after the British takeover.[^cite_note-337] Members of the [Malay community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Malays) speak [Sri Lanka Malay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Malay_language), a distinctive variety shaped by contact with the island's other languages.[^cite_note-338]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Sri_Lanka)

Religion in Sri Lanka (2024 census; percentages rounded)

[^cite_note-SL2024ReligionTable-339]

1. [Buddhism](1164480.md) (69.8%)

2. [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Sri_Lanka) (12.6%)

3. [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Sri_Lanka) (10.7%)

4. [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Sri_Lanka) (6.92%)

5. Other (0.02%)

[Buddhism](3267529.md) is Sri Lanka's largest religion and holds a constitutionally privileged position. Article 9 of the Constitution accords it the "foremost place" and requires the state to protect and foster the Buddha Sasana, while assuring all religions the rights provided by Articles 10 and 14(1)(e). These provisions protect freedom of thought, conscience and religion, and the freedom to manifest religion or belief.[^cite_note-SLConstitutionDemographics-336]

At the 2024 census, 69.8% of the population identified as Buddhist.[^cite_note-SL2024ReligionTable-339] Most Sri Lankan Buddhists belong to the [Sinhalese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhalese_people) ethnic group and follow the [Theravada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theravada) tradition.[^cite_note-SLReligiousGroups2025-340] According to Sri Lankan Buddhist chronicles, Buddhism was formally introduced to the island in the third century BCE by [Mahinda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahinda_\(Buddhist_monk\)), the son of Emperor [Ashoka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashoka), during the reign of King [Devanampiya Tissa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devanampiya_Tissa).[^cite_note-PereraBuddhismHistory-341] A cutting of the [Bodhi Tree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bodhi_Tree), under which [the Buddha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Buddha) is believed to have attained enlightenment, was brought to Sri Lanka by [Sanghamitta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanghamitta) during the same century.[^cite_note-342] The [Pāli Canon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P%C4%81li_Canon) (*Tipiṭaka*), previously transmitted orally, was committed to writing at [Aluvihara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aluvihare_Rock_Temple) around 30 BCE.[^cite_note-343] Sri Lanka has a continuous Buddhist tradition spanning more than two millennia. During periods of decline, its monastic ordination lineages were revived through contact with Thailand and Burma.[^cite_note-PereraBuddhismHistory-341]

[Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Sri_Lanka) form the country's largest religious minority, accounting for 12.6% of the population in 2024.[^cite_note-SL2024ReligionTable-339] Hindu traditions have been present on the island since at least the second century BCE.[^cite_note-344] Religious and cultural contacts across the [Palk Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palk_Strait) contributed to Hinduism's development in Sri Lanka. [Shaivism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaivism), centred on devotion to [Shiva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiva), has been a major tradition among Tamil Hindus and has strongly influenced the island's Hindu temple architecture and philosophy. The Tamil poet-saint [Thirugnanasambanthar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sambandar) referred to Sri Lankan Hindu temples in his hymns.[^cite_note-345]

[Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Sri_Lanka) is the country's third-largest religion, accounting for 10.7% of the population in 2024.[^cite_note-SL2024ReligionTable-339] It spread to the island through maritime trade, including contacts with Arab merchants. Most Sri Lankan Muslims are [Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Islam), and the [Shafi'i school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shafi%CA%BDi_school) has traditionally predominated.[^cite_note-346] The ancestry of the [Sri Lankan Moors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Moors) is associated with generations of Muslim trading communities and intermarriage with local populations.[^cite_note-347]



[Buddhist monks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhikkhu) in Sri Lanka

[Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Sri_Lanka) was present in Sri Lanka before European colonial rule. Traditions linking its origins to the first-century mission of [Thomas the Apostle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_the_Apostle) remain unconfirmed.[^cite_note-348] In the sixth century, [Cosmas Indicopleustes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cosmas_Indicopleustes) described a community of Persian Christians on the island.[^cite_note-349] Christianity became more widespread under European colonial rule, beginning with the arrival of the Portuguese in the early 16th century.[^cite_note-350] In 2024, Christians accounted for 6.9% of the population: 5.6% were [Roman Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Sri_Lanka) and 1.3% belonged to other Christian denominations.[^cite_note-SL2024ReligionTable-339] Catholic traditions in the country reflect the work of Portuguese missionaries, while Tamil Catholic communities also associate their religious heritage with [St. Francis Xavier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_Xavier).[^cite_note-351] Other Christian communities include the [Anglican Church of Ceylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_Ceylon), Methodists, Baptists, the Dutch Reformed Church and Pentecostal denominations.[^cite_note-SLReligiousGroups2025-340]

Sri Lanka also has a small [Zoroastrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoroastrian) community descended from [Parsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parsis) immigrants from India who settled in Ceylon during British rule.[^cite_note-352] A 2000 report described the community's declining numbers.[^cite_note-353]

Religion plays a prominent role in Sri Lankan life and culture. Buddhists observe monthly [Poya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poya) days, which fall on the full moon, while Hindus, Muslims and Christians observe their respective religious festivals. In [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup_poll) results published in 2025, 97% of Sri Lankan respondents said that religion was an important part of their daily lives.[^cite_note-354]

### Education

Main article: [Education in Sri Lanka](4560910.md)



The [Sri Lanka Institute of Nanotechnology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Institute_of_Nanotechnology), a research institute specialising in [nanotechnology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanotechnology)

Sri Lanka has a high level of [literacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literacy). The 2024 census recorded a literacy rate of 97.4% among people aged 10 and over, with rates of 97.9% for males and 97.0% for females.[^cite_note-SLLiteracy2024-355] Youth literacy was 98.8% in 2015,[^cite_note-356] while UNICEF's 2021 education sector budget brief reported a primary school enrolment rate of 99%.[^cite_note-357] Computer literacy among people aged 5–69 was estimated at 35.9% in 2024; the corresponding digital literacy rate was 65.0%.[^cite_note-358] [Compulsory education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compulsory_education) extends to age 16.[^cite_note-359]

The [free education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_education) system, introduced in 1945,[^cite_note-360] developed through the initiatives of [C. W. W. Kannangara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C._W._W._Kannangara) and A. Ratnayake.[^cite_note-autogenerated1-361][^cite_note-362] Publicly funded education extends from primary schooling to university study, although places at state universities are limited.[^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364] Kannangara promoted the establishment of [Madhya Vidyalayas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madhya_Vidyalaya) (central schools) to expand educational opportunities for rural children. A special committee on education presented its report in 1943, proposing reforms to broaden access to education.[^cite_note-365]

Following the establishment of provincial councils in 1987, responsibility for most government schools was transferred to provincial education authorities. [National schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_school_\(Sri_Lanka\)) remained under the central [Ministry of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_\(Sri_Lanka\)), while provincial schools were administered by the provincial authorities.[^cite_note-366] In 2024, official statistics recorded 10,076 government schools, 95 government-assisted private schools and 821 [pirivenas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pirivena).[^cite_note-367]

In 2024, 17 universities operated under the [University Grants Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_Grants_Commission_\(Sri_Lanka\)).[^cite_note-368] A 2009 country report identified mismatches between education and labour market needs, unequal access to quality education, and weak links between secondary and tertiary education as major challenges.[^cite_note-369] Private degree-awarding institutions also provide higher education, including institutions recognised under Section 25A of the Universities Act.[^cite_note-370] The gross tertiary enrolment ratio was 22% in 2020, according to the World Bank.[^cite_note-371] Sri Lanka ranked 93rd in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-372]

### Health

Main article: [Health in Sri Lanka](59153445.md)



Life expectancy at birth in Sri Lanka over time

The [World Health Organization](33583.md) estimated [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy) at birth in Sri Lanka at 77.2 years in 2021.[^cite_note-373] The Ministry of Health reported an infant mortality rate of 10.2 deaths per 1,000 live births in 2024, based on the Family Health Bureau's electronic reporting system. The maternal mortality ratio was 25 deaths per 100,000 live births in 2023.[^cite_note-SLHealthBulletin2024-374] Sri Lanka's public healthcare system provides preventive and curative services free of charge, including medicines and investigations.[^cite_note-375] WHO has identified this network of free public health services as an important contributor to improvements in the population's health.[^cite_note-376]

[Suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_in_Sri_Lanka) remains a public health concern. Police statistics recorded a rate of approximately 15 deaths per 100,000 people in 2022, with a higher rate among men than women. According to WHO, suicide rates have declined substantially since the 1990s following restrictions on highly hazardous pesticides, but remain above the global average. WHO describes the contributing factors as complex, including economic insecurity, unemployment, alcohol misuse, domestic violence and untreated mental health conditions.[^cite_note-377]

Sri Lanka was verified as having eliminated endemic [measles](58911.md) in 2019 and [rubella](172323.md) in 2020, ahead of the regional 2023 target.[^cite_note-378] A measles outbreak began in 2023, prompting supplementary vaccination campaigns in 2024. In 2024, the regional verification commission reclassified Sri Lanka as a country with re-established measles transmission.[^cite_note-379]

A preliminary study published in 2018, using 2015 surveillance data, found geographical clustering of reported [sexually transmitted infections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexually_transmitted_infection) (STIs), including [syphilis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syphilis) and [genital herpes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genital_herpes). The authors reported an association between districts with higher STI incidence and concentrations of populations at higher risk of infection, including around tourist destinations.[^cite_note-380]

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Sri_Lanka)



Hindu devotees engaging in *[Kavadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kavadi)* at a temple in [Vavuniya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vavuniya)

The culture of Sri Lanka is influenced primarily by Buddhism and Hinduism.[^cite_note-precol-381] Sri Lanka is the home to two main traditional cultures: the Sinhalese (centred in Kandy and Anuradhapura) and the Tamil (centred in Jaffna). Tamils co-existed with the Sinhalese people since then, and the early mixing rendered the two ethnic groups almost physically indistinguishable.[^cite_note-382] Ancient Sri Lanka is marked for its genius in [hydraulic engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_constructions_of_Sri_Lanka#Irrigation_Works) and [architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_ancient_Sri_Lanka). The British colonial culture has also influenced the locals. The rich cultural traditions shared by all Sri Lankan cultures is the basis of the country's long life expectancy, advanced health standards, and high literacy rate.[^cite_note-nub94-383]

### Visual, literary and performing arts

Main articles: [Cinema of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Sri_Lanka), [Music of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Sri_Lanka), [Dances of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dances_of_Sri_Lanka), [Theatre of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatre_of_Sri_Lanka), and [Sri Lankan literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_literature)



Female dancers in traditional [Kandyan dress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kandyan_dance)



The [Nelum Pokuna Mahinda Rajapaksa Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelum_Pokuna_Mahinda_Rajapaksa_Theatre) was constructed as a major venue for the performing arts.

The movie *[Kadawunu Poronduwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadawunu_Poronduwa)* (*The Broken Promise*), produced by [S. M. Nayagam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._M._Nayagam) of Chitra Kala Movietone, heralded the coming of Sri Lankan cinema in 1947. *[Ranmuthu Duwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranmuthu_Duwa)* (*Island of Treasures*) marked the transition of cinema from [black-and-white](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black-and-white) to colour. In recent years, movies have featured subjects such as family melodrama, social transformation and the years of conflict between the military and the LTTE.[^cite_note-easiancinem-384] The Sri Lankan cinematic style is similar to [Bollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bollywood) movies. In 1979, movie attendance rose to an all-time high but has been in a steady decline since then.[^cite_note-denandind-385]

An influential filmmaker is [Lester James Peiris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lester_James_Peiris), who has directed a number of movies which led to global acclaim, including *[Rekava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rekava)* (*Line of Destiny*, 1956), *[Gamperaliya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gamperaliya_\(film\))* (*The Changing Village*, 1964), *[Nidhanaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nidhanaya)* (*The Treasure*, 1970) and *[Golu Hadawatha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golu_Hadawatha)* (*Cold Heart*, 1968).[^cite_note-lstjms-386] Sri Lankan-Canadian poet [Rienzi Crusz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rienzi_Crusz), is the subject of a documentary on his life in Sri Lanka. His work is published in Sinhala and English. Naturalised Canadian [Michael Ondaatje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Ondaatje) is well known for his English-language novels and three films.

The earliest music in Sri Lanka came from theatrical performances such as *Kolam*, *Sokari* and *Nadagam*.[^cite_note-cambrmu-387] Traditional music instruments such as *Béra*, *Thammátama*, *Daŭla* and *Răbān* were performed at these dramas. The first music album, *Nurthi*, recorded in 1903, was released through Radio Ceylon. Songwriters like [Mahagama Sekara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahagama_Sekara) and [Ananda Samarakoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ananda_Samarakoon) and musicians such as [W. D. Amaradeva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W._D._Amaradeva), [Victor Ratnayake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Ratnayake), [Nanda Malini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanda_Malini) and [Clarence Wijewardene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarence_Wijewardene) have contributed much towards the progression of Sri Lankan music.[^cite_note-wmrhg-388] [Baila music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baila_music) originated among [Kaffirs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Kaffirs) or the Afro-Sinhalese community.[^cite_note-lnlypnlu-389]



A Low Country drummer playing the traditional *Yak Béra*

There are three main styles of Sri Lankan classical dance. They are, the [Kandyan dances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kandyan_dance), low country dances and [Sabaragamuwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabaragamuwa) dances. Of these, the Kandyan style is most prominent. It is a sophisticated form of dance[^cite_note-dncen2-390] that consists of five sub-categories: *Ves dance*, *Naiyandi dance*, *Udekki dance*, *Pantheru dance* and *18 Vannam*.[^cite_note-sldance-391] An elaborate headdress is worn by the male dancers, and a drum called *Geta Béraya* is used to assist the dancers to keep on rhythm.[^cite_note-autogenerated3-392]

The history of Sri Lankan painting and sculpture can be traced as far back as to the 2nd or 3rd century BCE.[^cite_note-lklibrform-393] The earliest mention about the art of painting on Mahāvaṃsa, is to the drawing of a palace on cloth using [cinnabar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinnabar) in the 2nd century BCE. The chronicles have a description of various paintings in relic chambers of Buddhist stupas and in monastic residences.

Theatre came to the country when a [Parsi theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parsi_theatre) company from [Mumbai](19189.md) introduced *Nurti*, a blend of European and Indian theatrical conventions to the Colombo audience in the 19th century.[^cite_note-sldance-391] The golden age of Sri Lankan drama and theatre began with the staging of *Maname*, a play written by [Ediriweera Sarachchandra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ediriweera_Sarachchandra) in 1956.[^cite_note-theatresl-394] It was followed by a series of popular dramas like *Sinhabāhu*, *Pabāvatī*, *Mahāsāra*, *Muudu Puththu* and *Subha saha Yasa*.

Sri Lankan literature spans at least two millennia and is heir to the [Aryan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aryan_race) literary tradition as embodied in the hymns of the [Rigveda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rigveda).[^cite_note-lklinbr-395] The [Pāli Canon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P%C4%81li_Canon), the standard collection of scriptures in the Theravada Buddhist tradition, was written down in Sri Lanka during the [Fourth Buddhist council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Buddhist_council), at the Alulena cave temple, [Kegalle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kegalle), as early as 29 BCE.[^cite_note-alulena-396] Chronicles such as the *[Mahāvaṃsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mah%C4%81va%E1%B9%83sa)*, written in the 6th century, provide vivid descriptions of Sri Lankan dynasties. According to the German philosopher [Wilhelm Geiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelm_Geiger), the chronicles are based on Sinhala [Atthakatha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atthakatha) (commentary).[^cite_note-lklinbr-395] The oldest surviving prose work is the *Dhampiya-Atuva-Getapadaya*, compiled in the 9th century CE.[^cite_note-lklinbr-395] The greatest literary feats of mediaeval Sri Lanka include *Sandesha Kāvya* (poetic messages) such as *Girā Sandeshaya* (parrot message), *Hansa Sandeshaya* (swan message) and *Salalihini Sandeshaya* (myna message). Poetry including *Kavsilumina*, *Kavya-Sekharaya* (*Diadem of Poetry*) and proses such as *Saddharma-Ratnāvaliya*, *Amāvatura* (*Flood of Nectar*) and *Pujāvaliya* are also notable works of this period, which is considered to be the golden age of Sri Lankan literature.[^cite_note-lklinbr-395] The first modern-day novel, *Meena* by Simon de Silva appeared in 1905[^cite_note-sldance-391] and was followed by several revolutionary literary works. [Martin Wickramasinghe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Wickramasinghe), the author of *[Madol Doova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madol_Doova)* is considered the iconic figure of Sri Lankan literature.[^cite_note-martinw-397]

### Media

Main article: [Media in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Sri_Lanka)

The [Sri Lanka Broadcasting Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Broadcasting_Corporation) (formerly [Radio Ceylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Ceylon)) is the oldest-running radio station in Asia,[^cite_note-slbcv-398] established in 1923 by [Edward Harper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Harper_\(engineer\)), just three years after broadcasting began in Europe.[^cite_note-slbcv-398] The station broadcasts services in Sinhala, Tamil, English and Hindi. Since the 1980s, many private radio stations have also been introduced. Broadcast television was introduced in 1979 when the [Independent Television Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_Television_Network) was launched. Initially, all television stations were state-controlled, but private television networks began broadcasting in 1992.[^cite_note-399]

As of 2020, 192 newspapers (122 Sinhala, 24 Tamil, 43 English, 3 multilingual) are published and 25 TV stations and 58 radio stations are in operation.[^cite_note-Annual_Report_2020-291] In recent years, [freedom of the press](105323.md) in Sri Lanka has been alleged by media freedom groups to be amongst the [poorest in democratic countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Press_Freedom_Index).[^cite_note-400] Alleged abuse of a newspaper editor by a senior government minister[^cite_note-You_are_pigs_who_eat_shit-401] achieved international notoriety because of the unsolved murder of the editor's predecessor, [Lasantha Wickrematunge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lasantha_Wickrematunge),[^cite_note-Death_foretold-402] who had been a critic of the government and had presaged his own death in a posthumously published article.[^cite_note-pre-written_posthumous_editorial-403]

### Cuisine and festivals

Main articles: [Sri Lankan cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_cuisine) and [Festivals in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Festivals_in_Sri_Lanka)



Sri Lankan rice and curry

Dishes include rice and curry, [pittu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puttu), [kiribath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiribath), wholemeal [roti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roti), [string hoppers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idiyappam), [watalappam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watalappam) (a rich pudding of Malay origin made with [coconut milk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut_milk), jaggery, cashews, eggs, and spices including cinnamon and nutmeg), [kottu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kottu), and [appam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appam). [Jackfruit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jackfruit) may sometimes replace rice. Traditionally food is served on a [plantain leaf or lotus leaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plantain_\(cooking\)). Middle Eastern influences and practices are found in traditional Moor dishes, while Dutch and Portuguese influences are found with the island's Burgher community preserving their culture through traditional dishes such as [lamprais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamprais) (rice cooked in stock and baked in a banana leaf), [breudher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breudher) (Dutch holiday biscuit), and [Bolo fiado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolo_fiado) (Portuguese-style layer cake).

In April, Sri Lankans celebrate the [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinhalese_New_Year) and [Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_New_Year) new year festivals.[^cite_note-dersl-404] [Esala Perahera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esala_Perahera) is a symbolic Buddhist festival consisting of dances and decorated elephants held in Kandy in July and August.[^cite_note-esalasl-405] Fire dances, whip dances, Kandyan dances and various other cultural dances are integral parts of the festival. Christians celebrate [Christmas](6237.md) on 25 December to celebrate the birth of Jesus Christ and [Easter](9325.md) to celebrate the resurrection of Jesus. Tamils celebrate [Thai Pongal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thai_Pongal) and [Maha Shivaratri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maha_Shivaratri), and Muslims celebrate [Hajj](23487999.md) and [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan).

### Sport

Main article: [Sport in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Sri_Lanka)



[R. Premadasa Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R._Premadasa_Stadium) in [Colombo](56636.md)

While the [national sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_sport) is [volleyball](32558.md), by far the most popular sport in the country is [cricket](25675557.md).[^cite_note-406] [Rugby union](25405.md) also enjoys extensive popularity,[^cite_note-407] as do [association football](10568.md), [netball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netball) and [tennis](29773.md). Aquatic sports such as boating, surfing, swimming, kitesurfing[^cite_note-408] and [scuba diving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuba_diving) attract many Sri Lankans and foreign tourists. There are two styles of martial arts native to Sri Lanka: [Cheena di](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheena_di) and [Angampora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angampora).[^cite_note-409]

Muttiah Muralitharan

[Muttiah Muralitharan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muttiah_Muralitharan) holds the record for the highest number of [wickets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wicket#Dismissing_a_batsman) in [Test cricket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Test_cricket).

[^cite_note-Muralitharan-410]

The [Sri Lanka national cricket team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_national_cricket_team) achieved considerable success beginning in the 1990s, rising from [underdog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Underdog) status to winning the [1996 Cricket World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Cricket_World_Cup), defeating [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_national_cricket_team) in the final on 17 March 1996.[^cite_note-411] They also won the [2014 ICC World Twenty20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_World_Twenty20) played in Bangladesh, beating India in the final. In addition, Sri Lanka became the runners-up of the [Cricket World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cricket_World_Cup) in [2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Cricket_World_Cup)[^cite_note-412] and [2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Cricket_World_Cup_final),[^cite_note-413] and of the [ICC World Twenty20](8258172.md) in [2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_World_Twenty20) and [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_World_Twenty20).[^cite_note-414] Former Sri Lankan [off-spinner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Off_spin) [Muttiah Muralitharan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muttiah_Muralitharan) has been rated as the greatest [test match](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Test_cricket) [bowler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bowler_\(cricket\)) ever by *[Wisden Cricketers' Almanack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisden_Cricketers'_Almanack)*,[^cite_note-415] and four Sri Lankan cricketers ranked 2nd ([Sangakkara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kumar_Sangakkara)), 4th ([Jayasuriya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanath_Jayasuriya)), 5th ([Jayawardene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahela_Jayawardene)) and 11th ([Dilshan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tillakaratne_Dilshan)) highest ODI run scorers of all time, which is the second best by a team. As of June 2022, Muttiah Muralitharan has the highest aggregate wickets in Test Cricket with a record 800 wickets, a feat he achieved in a Test Match against India in July 2010 that Sri Lanka had won by 10 Wickets.[^cite_note-416][^cite_note-417] Sri Lanka has won the [Asia Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia_Cup) in 1986,[^cite_note-418] 1997,[^cite_note-419] 2004,[^cite_note-420] 2008,[^cite_note-421] 2014.[^cite_note-422] and 2022.[^cite_note-423] Sri Lanka once held the highest team score in all three formats of cricket.[^cite_note-424] The country co-hosted the Cricket World Cup in 1996 and [2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Cricket_World_Cup) and hosted the 2012 ICC World Twenty20.

Sri Lankans have won two medals at [Olympic Games](22576.md): one silver, by [Duncan White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duncan_White) at the [1948 London Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Summer_Olympics) for men's [400 metres hurdles](707859.md);[^cite_note-425] and one silver by [Susanthika Jayasinghe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susanthika_Jayasinghe) at the [2000 Sydney Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_Summer_Olympics) for women's [200 metres](1833981.md).[^cite_note-426] In 1973, [Muhammad Lafir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Lafir) won the [World Billiards Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBSF_World_Billiards_Championship), the highest feat by a Sri Lankan in a [Cue sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cue_sports).[^cite_note-427] Sri Lanka has also won the Carrom World Championship titles twice in 2012, [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Carrom_World_Championship)[^cite_note-428] and 2018, the men's team becoming champions and the women's team winning second place. The [Sri Lankan National Badminton Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_National_Badminton_Championships) was annually held between 1953 and 2011.

Sri Lanka is ranked 6th in the ICC Men's Test Team Rankings 2025 with rating 86.[^cite_note-429]

[Sri Lanka national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_national_football_team) also won the prestigious [1995 South Asian Gold Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_SAARC_Gold_Cup).[^cite_note-430][^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432][^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434]

Retrieved 5 September 2021. By diverting the attention of policymakers towards pointless nonscientific arguments instead of promoting such integrated management systems and high technological fertilizer production, will be only a time-wasting effort and meanwhile, the global demand for Ceylon Tea will generate diminishing returns. At present, there are about 500,000 direct beneficiaries from the tea industry and about 600 factories are operating around the country. In general, the livelihood of about 3 million people is directly and indirectly woven around the domestic tea industry. The researchers and the experienced growers have predicted that a 50 percent reduction in the yield has to be anticipated with the ban of chemical fertilizer. The negative implication of this yield reduction is such that there is a risk of collapsing the banking sector which is centralized around the tea industry in the major tea growing areas including Ratnapura, Galle, Matara, Kaluthara, and Kegalle.
